1.  **LD Prompt Agent:** An LLM that optimizes prompts for LD to generate high-quality foreground instance images and segmentation masks with single objects and clean backgrounds, refined using TextGrad.
2.  **Data Validation Agent:** A VLLM that filters out low-quality synthetic images, also with system prompts refined by TextGrad.

The framework also uses image harmonization to combine multiple instances within scenes. Experimental results show improvements in performance for YOLOv9 and YOLO11 families on COCO and LVIS datasets, particularly in rare classes and open-vocabulary object detection, compared to state-of-the-art methods like MosaicFusion.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of using an agent-based approach to data generation, leveraging LLMs and VLLMs with a focus on prompt optimization and quality validation, has some novelty.  TextGrad-refined prompts is a fairly interesting feature. However, the individual components (LLMs for text generation, VLLMs for image validation, diffusion models for image generation) are not new *per se*.  The novelty lies in the specific *combination* and *orchestration* of these existing technologies. Prior work has used LLMs and diffusion models separately for data augmentation. The tight coupling of LLMs for prompt engineering and VLLMs for validation within a closed-loop system appears to be a stronger contribution. The prompt optimization using TextGrad is a notable addition.
*   **Significance:**  The demonstrated improvements in object detection and instance segmentation, particularly for rare classes and open-vocabulary scenarios, are significant.  The gains over baselines like MosaicFusion and YOLO-Worldv2-M are noteworthy. The method improves upon existing approaches in synthetic data generation by reducing invalid data and improving mask quality. The demonstrated improvements in established YOLO models on standard datasets indicate the potential of Gen-n-Val to impact practical applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The agentic framework provides a structured and automated approach to data generation.
    *   Prompt optimization and data validation steps enhance the quality of synthetic data.
    *   The method is shown to improve performance on challenging benchmarks, especially for rare classes.
    *   Clear presentation of the framework and experimental results.
    * TextGrad refinement of both LD and VLLM prompts is a strong architectural addition.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies on existing LLMs, VLLMs and diffusion models. While the integration is novel, the individual components are not.
    *   The computational cost of running LLMs for prompt optimization and VLLMs for data validation could be a limitation, although this is not explicitly addressed.
    *   Contextual coherence in instance placement is mentioned as a limitation, suggesting potential for further improvements. There appears to still be a degree of randomness to image generation.
    *   Limited generalizability beyond the tested models and datasets. The experiments primarily focus on YOLO families and COCO/LVIS datasets.

*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   Gen-n-Val could become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working on object detection and instance segmentation, especially when dealing with limited real data or class imbalance issues.
    *   The agentic approach could inspire further research on automated data generation and augmentation techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the analysis above, Gen-n-Val offers a novel combination of existing technologies to tackle a practical problem in computer vision.  It presents a structured agent-based framework that demonstrably improves upon previous approaches.  However, it is not a groundbreaking theoretical breakthrough. The dependence on external LLM/VLLM services presents a potential bottleneck regarding computational cost and wider adoption. The limitations regarding contextual coherence indicate areas for future research. Therefore, the novelty resides in clever application of existing tools rather than core innovation in data generation.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Normative Conflicts and Shallow AI Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04679v1)
- **Authors**: Raphaël Millière
- **Abstract**: The progress of AI systems such as large language models (LLMs) raises increasingly pressing concerns about their safe deployment. This paper examines the value alignment problem for LLMs, arguing that current alignment strategies are fundamentally inadequate to prevent misuse. Despite ongoing efforts to instill norms such as helpfulness, honesty, and harmlessness in LLMs through fine-tuning based on human preferences, they remain vulnerable to adversarial attacks that exploit conflicts between these norms. I argue that this vulnerability reflects a fundamental limitation of existing alignment methods: they reinforce shallow behavioral dispositions rather than endowing LLMs with a genuine capacity for normative deliberation. Drawing from on research in moral psychology, I show how humans' ability to engage in deliberative reasoning enhances their resilience against similar adversarial tactics. LLMs, by contrast, lack a robust capacity to detect and rationally resolve normative conflicts, leaving them susceptible to manipulation; even recent advances in reasoning-focused LLMs have not addressed this vulnerability. This ``shallow alignment'' problem carries significant implications for AI safety and regulation, suggesting that current approaches are insufficient for mitigating potential harms posed by increasingly capable AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Normative Conflicts and Shallow AI Alignment":

**Summary:**

The paper argues that current approaches to aligning large language models (LLMs) with human values are fundamentally inadequate to prevent misuse. It claims that these approaches, which rely on techniques like preference fine-tuning, only create "shallow alignment" by reinforcing superficial behavioral dispositions rather than endowing LLMs with genuine normative reasoning capabilities. The author demonstrates that LLMs are vulnerable to adversarial attacks that exploit conflicts between different alignment norms (helpfulness, honesty, harmlessness). Because LLMs lack the capacity for normative deliberation to rationally resolve these conflicts, they are susceptible to manipulation through carefully crafted prompts. The paper contrasts this with human resilience, which is supported by normative reasoning capacities. The author identifies a novel vulnerability of Reasoning Language Models to Thought Injection Attacks. The conclusion is that these limitations have significant implications for AI safety and regulation, suggesting that current strategies are insufficient to mitigate potential harms from increasingly capable AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper brings several novel elements to the discourse around AI alignment. First, the argument explicitly links adversarial prompt generation, normative conflicts, and the shallowness of LLM alignment. Second, the paper frames the challenge as one of normative *deliberation* instead of just value enforcement, which is a more sophisticated way of analyzing the problem. Third, the paper introduces the novel "thought injection attack", which is a substantial contribution because of the new type of vulnerability it exposes.

* **Significance:** The paper's argument has significant implications for the field. It challenges the prevailing view that scaling up current alignment strategies will solve the problem of LLM misuse. The diagnosis that LLMs lack genuine normative reasoning and are thus vulnerable to manipulation is particularly important. The paper's argument could influence future research directions, prompting researchers to explore more robust alignment methods that go beyond superficial behavior reinforcement. The introduction of thought injection attacks highlights a critical vulnerability in RLMs and motivates the development of new defense strategies.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured argument.
    *   It integrates insights from moral psychology to explain the difference between human and LLM responses to normative conflicts.
    *   The examples of adversarial attacks are compelling and illustrate the limitations of current alignment methods effectively.
    *   The paper offers a diagnosis, not just a description, of the problem. The diagnosis is insightful.
    *   The introduction of a new category of attack (thought injection) is highly valuable.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper does not present a fully elaborated alternative solution. While it hints at the need for systems that can engage in explicit normative deliberation, it does not provide specific technical strategies for achieving this.
    *   It assumes that it is clear which alignment policies are correct in a given situation, or how a LLM would have the capacity to decide, which may or may not be simple in real world scenarios.
    *   The paper's argumentation is somewhat reliant on the specific definition of *shallow*, whose definition may be questioned. It is possible that existing alignment techniques are not as simplistic as the paper suggests.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field. It highlights a critical vulnerability in LLMs that has been relatively overlooked. It challenges the prevailing paradigm of alignment through superficial behavior reinforcement, prompting researchers to explore more robust and nuanced approaches. The argument is likely to be cited by researchers working on AI safety and regulation. In particular, "thought injection attacks" could become a primary focus of research.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a significant contribution to the field of AI safety, but it has clear limits, as noted above. It offers a novel perspective on the value alignment problem for LLMs, exposes a critical vulnerability, and challenges prevailing assumptions. While it does not present a complete solution, its insightful diagnosis and proposed shift in focus toward normative deliberation are likely to stimulate further research and innovation in this critical area.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MARS: Radio Map Super-resolution and Reconstruction Method under Sparse Channel Measurements
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04682v1)
- **Authors**: Chuyun Deng, Na Liu, Wei Xie, Lianming Xu, Li Wang
- **Abstract**: Radio maps reflect the spatial distribution of signal strength and are essential for applications like smart cities, IoT, and wireless network planning. However, reconstructing accurate radio maps from sparse measurements remains challenging. Traditional interpolation and inpainting methods lack environmental awareness, while many deep learning approaches depend on detailed scene data, limiting generalization. To address this, we propose MARS, a Multi-scale Aware Radiomap Super-resolution method that combines CNNs and Transformers with multi-scale feature fusion and residual connections. MARS focuses on both global and local feature extraction, enhancing feature representation across different receptive fields and improving reconstruction accuracy. Experiments across different scenes and antenna locations show that MARS outperforms baseline models in both MSE and SSIM, while maintaining low computational cost, demonstrating strong practical potential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MARS: Radio Map Super-resolution and Reconstruction Method under Sparse Channel Measurements" proposes a novel deep learning approach, MARS, for reconstructing high-resolution radio maps from sparse signal strength measurements. It addresses the challenge of accurately estimating radio map information when only limited data points are available. MARS combines convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and Transformers, employing a multi-scale feature fusion technique and residual connections. The CNN component focuses on local feature extraction, while the Transformer captures global dependencies. A high-frequency filtering module is included to enhance feature extraction, and a Pyramid-Aware UBlock (PAU) facilitates multi-scale feature separation.  A tailored loss function incorporates gradient information and building structure to improve reconstruction accuracy, particularly in complex urban environments. The paper evaluates MARS against several baseline methods across different experimental settings, demonstrating superior performance in terms of MSE and SSIM metrics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Relevance:** Reconstructing radio maps from sparse measurements is a significant challenge with practical implications for wireless network planning, smart city deployments, and IoT applications.
*   **Methodological Novelty:** The combination of CNNs and Transformers in MARS is a reasonable architectural choice for this problem. CNNs excel at capturing local spatial information and high frequency components, while Transformers are effective at capturing long-range dependencies, both important for RF propagation modeling. The incorporation of a HFM module strengthens the approach further. The PAU design is well-motivated by the scale-dependent nature of RF signal propagation.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The paper conducts thorough experiments with two distinct settings, demonstrating the model's performance in both unseen transmitter locations and unseen environments. The comparison against several baselines, including both traditional methods and other deep learning approaches, provides a good benchmark. The loss function design demonstrates an attention to detail.
*   **Performance:** MARS consistently outperforms baseline methods in both quantitative metrics (MSE, SSIM) and qualitative (visual) assessment.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of CNNs and Transformers is well-motivated, similar approaches have been explored in other image super-resolution and inpainting tasks. The specific architecture details (e.g., layer configurations, attention mechanisms) may not represent substantial innovations over existing literature. It's important to assess to what degree this work is simply an adaptation of existing techniques to a new domain. It's not clear if there were any particularly interesting architectural choices made.
*   **Limited Generalizability:** The experimental validation is based on the RadioMapSeer dataset, which uses simulated radio maps generated by Winprop. While this is a reasonable starting point, the performance of MARS in real-world environments with more complex and unpredictable signal propagation patterns needs to be demonstrated. Simulations may not fully capture all the nuances of real-world radio environments. The paper might also consider including more complex evaluation metrics like positioning error using the learned radio maps.
*   **Clarity of Technical Details:** There's room for improvement in the clarity of technical descriptions.  For example, some readers may find the explanation of the loss function components slightly brief.
*   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** The paper would be stronger with ablation studies to evaluate the contribution of individual components (e.g., the multi-scale feature fusion module, the gradient loss) to the overall performance of MARS. This would provide deeper insights into the effectiveness of each design choice. Without them, it is difficult to determine what really drives performance.

**Significance:**

MARS demonstrates a promising approach for radio map reconstruction from sparse measurements. Its effectiveness in simulated environments suggests potential for real-world applications. The combination of CNNs and Transformers, along with the custom loss function, represents a reasonable architectural approach for this problem.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7/10**.

*   The paper addresses an important and relevant problem with practical implications.
*   The proposed method, MARS, demonstrates strong performance compared to existing methods in simulated environments.
*   The combination of CNNs and Transformers is a sound architectural design choice for this task, and the inclusion of multi-scale feature fusion and building-aware loss further enhances the approach.

However, there are some notable weaknesses:

*   The methodological novelty is incremental, as similar architectures have been explored in other image super-resolution tasks.
*   The experimental validation is limited to simulated data, and the generalizability to real-world environments remains uncertain.
*   The paper could benefit from more detailed technical descriptions and ablation studies to provide deeper insights into the effectiveness of individual components. Without the ablation studies, it is difficult to ascertain what truly drives the performance improvement.

Therefore, while the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field, its impact is somewhat limited by the aforementioned weaknesses. Further research is needed to demonstrate the real-world effectiveness of MARS and to explore more novel architectural designs.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### MMRefine: Unveiling the Obstacles to Robust Refinement in Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04688v1)
- **Authors**: Gio Paik, Geewook Kim, Jinbae Im
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces MMRefine, a MultiModal Refinement benchmark designed to evaluate the error refinement capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). As the emphasis shifts toward enhancing reasoning during inference, MMRefine provides a framework that evaluates MLLMs' abilities to detect and correct errors across six distinct scenarios beyond just comparing final accuracy before and after refinement. Furthermore, the benchmark analyzes the refinement performance by categorizing errors into six error types. Experiments with various open and closed MLLMs reveal bottlenecks and factors impeding refinement performance, highlighting areas for improvement in effective reasoning enhancement. Our code and dataset are publicly available at https://github.com/naver-ai/MMRefine.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MMRefine: Unveiling the Obstacles to Robust Refinement in Multimodal Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMRefine, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the error refinement capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  Unlike prior work that focuses primarily on overall accuracy before and after refinement, MMRefine provides a more granular analysis by categorizing refinement outcomes into six distinct scenarios (False Error Detection, Verification Success, Refinement Failure, Error Detection Success, Error Correction Success, Refinement Success) and categorizes errors into six distinct error types (Problem understanding, Logical reasoning, Calculation, Equation, Visual perception, Spatial reasoning).  The authors evaluate several open and closed-source MLLMs, revealing performance bottlenecks and highlighting areas for improvement in effective reasoning enhancement. The benchmark dataset and code are publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *granularity* of the benchmark.  While self-refinement and iterative reasoning are established techniques, the detailed categorization of both error types and refinement outcomes is a significant step forward. This allows for a much deeper understanding of *where* and *why* MLLMs fail during refinement, rather than just knowing *that* they fail. This surpasses the previous focus on simply comparing final accuracies. Also, the paper generates initial solutions using 4 different MLLMs, and then uses these solutions for refinement, which is more realistic than artificially creating errors.

*   **Significance:** The significance of MMRefine is its potential to guide the development of more robust and reliable MLLMs. By identifying specific weaknesses in refinement capabilities across various scenarios and error types, the benchmark provides concrete targets for future research. Specifically, the insights on visual and spatial reasoning are a notable contribution. The comparison of models across different sizes and architectures offers practical information for developers. The insights from RefScore and the correlation with existing benchmarks offer a good method of knowing which models offer reasonable refinement performance relative to the increased computational cost of refinement, a very valuable insight.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Granular Evaluation:** The key strength is the six-scenario and six-error-type classification framework.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study evaluates a relatively large number of models (17).
    *   **Reproducibility:** Publicly available dataset and code promote reproducibility and future research.
    *   **Practical Insights:** The paper offers practical advice regarding model selection for refinement tasks, weighing performance against computational cost.
    *   **Use of LLMs for Evaluation:** The use of GPT-4o as a judge, with human verification, provides a scalable evaluation approach.
    *   **Analysis of the limitations of current models:** In particular, the fact that the performance on most error types is highly correlated with other error types, while the performance of spatial reasoning errors is not. This means that scaling up the refinement capability of MLLMs may not be a solution to enhance refinement performance for specific error types such as spatial reasoning errors.
    *   **Analysis of the limitations of closed-source vs. open-source models:** The analysis of both open-source and closed-source models show valuable insights on refinement ability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Math-Centric Focus:** The benchmark focuses exclusively on mathematical problems. While this allows for clear correctness judgments, it limits the generalizability of the findings to other domains (e.g., natural language understanding, creative writing).
    *   **Reliance on LLM-as-Judge:** Although validated with human evaluation, the reliance on GPT-4o introduces a potential bias and raises questions about the robustness of the evaluation. The level of agreement (72% and 73%) is not perfect.
    *   **Dataset Size:** The dataset size (200 problems) may be small compared to the scale of modern MLLMs.
    *   **Error-Type Annotation Subjectivity:** Human annotation is used to annotate the first error type in each initial solution. Annotating these error types can be highly subjective and time-consuming.
    *   **Lack of Analysis of Bias**: Some works show that smaller language models show bias and error with specific number formats and notation in math problem-solving (Nath et al. 2025). There is no discussion of bias due to number format or notation.
    *   **Lack of Discussion of Counter-Example Injection** The paper generates reference feedback using OpenAI 01, but there is no mention of counter-example injection during refinement. This is a critical and important technique for enhancing LLMs' reasoning during refinement.
    *   **No Analysis on Robustness**: The analysis does not account for perturbations in the prompt during the refinement process.

*   **Impact on the Field:** The paper is likely to have a moderate to significant impact. The MMRefine benchmark can become a standard tool for evaluating and comparing MLLMs' refinement capabilities. The identified bottlenecks can inspire new research directions in areas like visual reasoning, spatial reasoning, and error correction.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** MMRefine makes a valuable contribution by providing a more granular and targeted evaluation of MLLM refinement than previous benchmarks. This has the potential to significantly guide future research and development in this area. However, the limited domain (mathematics) and reliance on LLM-as-judge introduce certain limitations, lowering the score. The lack of bias analysis, counter-example injection and robustness also lowers the score. In essence, it's a solid and worthwhile contribution, but not groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher rating.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Recycling the Web: A Method to Enhance Pre-training Data Quality and Quantity for Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04689v1)
- **Authors**: Thao Nguyen, Yang Li, Olga Golovneva, Luke Zettlemoyer, Sewoong Oh, Ludwig Schmidt, Xian Li
- **Abstract**: Scaling laws predict that the performance of large language models improves with increasing model size and data size. In practice, pre-training has been relying on massive web crawls, using almost all data sources publicly available on the internet so far. However, this pool of natural data does not grow at the same rate as the compute supply. Furthermore, the availability of high-quality texts is even more limited: data filtering pipelines often remove up to 99% of the initial web scrapes to achieve state-of-the-art. To address the "data wall" of pre-training scaling, our work explores ways to transform and recycle data discarded in existing filtering processes. We propose REWIRE, REcycling the Web with guIded REwrite, a method to enrich low-quality documents so that they could become useful for training. This in turn allows us to increase the representation of synthetic data in the final pre-training set. Experiments at 1B, 3B and 7B scales of the DCLM benchmark show that mixing high-quality raw texts and our rewritten texts lead to 1.0, 1.3 and 2.5 percentage points improvement respectively across 22 diverse tasks, compared to training on only filtered web data. Training on the raw-synthetic data mix is also more effective than having access to 2x web data. Through further analysis, we demonstrate that about 82% of the mixed in texts come from transforming lower-quality documents that would otherwise be discarded. REWIRE also outperforms related approaches of generating synthetic data, including Wikipedia-style paraphrasing, question-answer synthesizing and knowledge extraction. These results suggest that recycling web texts holds the potential for being a simple and effective approach for scaling pre-training data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of scaling pre-training data for large language models (LLMs), focusing on the bottleneck of high-quality data. The authors propose a method called REWIRE (REcycling the Web with guided REwrite), which enriches lower-quality web documents that are typically discarded during filtering processes. REWIRE uses an LLM to identify the purpose of the document, then generate an improved version using chain-of-thought reasoning. The enhanced documents are then mixed with high-quality raw web data for pre-training. Experiments on the DCLM benchmark at 1B, 3B, and 7B scales demonstrate that mixing high-quality raw texts and rewritten texts leads to improvements compared to training on only filtered web data. The results suggest that a significant proportion of the mixed-in texts comes from transforming lower-quality documents that would otherwise be discarded. The paper also compares REWIRE to other synthetic data generation approaches, showing competitive or superior performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of recycling discarded web data is not entirely novel, as previous research has explored relaxing filtering criteria or using synthetic data generation. The novelty lies in the *specific approach* of using a large LLM for guided rewriting based on identified purpose and reasoning, and particularly focusing on *moderate* quality discards rather than relying entirely on high quality data. While prior synthetic data approaches exists, REWIRE is distinguished by the targeted augmentation of low-quality documents leveraging a strong LLM. The combination of recycling discarded texts with a guided rewriting process that incorporates chain-of-thought reasoning is a specific contribution to the field.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practically important problem: the scarcity of high-quality pre-training data. The results are compelling, demonstrating improvements in downstream performance on the DCLM benchmark. The finding that REWIRE can achieve comparable performance to using twice the amount of raw data is highly significant and has the potential to reduce the data demands of LLM pre-training. The ablations, overlap analyses and comparisons also reveal the value-add of REWIRE compared to using just reweighted or filtered data from conventional sources. The gains appear to hold over scale too.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation (data wall).
    *   Well-defined method (REWIRE) with detailed explanation.
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating performance improvements.
    *   Comprehensive comparisons to existing approaches.
    *   Analysis of the contribution of recycled vs. newly-generated data.
    *   Demonstration that rewritten data has high diversity comparable to high-quality raw data, and greater diversity compared to other data augmentation methods.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The cost of generating synthetic data using a large LLM can be significant, which could be a barrier to adoption. This point is acknowledged but might warrant further investigation of cost vs benefit.
    *   There is a risk of hallucination when using LLMs to rewrite documents. The paper makes an attempt to assess factuality but the risk needs more emphasis.
    *   The rewriting is done using Llama-3 which means this is inherently tied to the models quality. Is the rewriting dependent on a better model? This is not fully addressed.
    *   The study is limited to the DCLM benchmark, a relatively specific dataset, with the risk of overfitting this dataset or a lack of generalisability of the approach. Broader evaluations would strengthen the claims.
    *   Limited discussion is presented around why or when this works the best. It is stated the method can adapt/add new knowledge/content, but this process is not investigated.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of LLM pre-training by providing a practical method for augmenting training data. This is especially helpful at a time of growing awareness of diminishing returns, data shortages and the environmental impacts of training. If REWIRE can be made more efficient and scalable, it could become a standard technique in LLM training pipelines. It provides a viable strategy for better utilizing existing data resources.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

REWIRE presents a well-executed approach to data augmentation, addressing a clear bottleneck in LLM development with strong empirical backing. While the method relies on expensive LLM calls for rewriting, and risks hallucinations, and the evaluations are confined to one benchmark, the findings are promising and potentially impactful. The comparative analysis, combined with the ablations and overlap measures are good. However, given the risk of using existing data and models (e.g. Llama 3) to perform this method, and the single dataset benchmark, a higher score is not yet warranted.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Better Generalization via Distributional Input Projection Network
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04690v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Hao, Yanxin Lu, Xinwei Shen, Tong Zhang
- **Abstract**: As overparameterized models become increasingly prevalent, training loss alone offers limited insight into generalization performance. While smoothness has been linked to improved generalization across various settings, directly enforcing smoothness in neural networks remains challenging. To address this, we introduce Distributional Input Projection Networks (DIPNet), a novel framework that projects inputs into learnable distributions at each layer. This distributional representation induces a smoother loss landscape with respect to the input, promoting better generalization. We provide theoretical analysis showing that DIPNet reduces both local smoothness measures and the Lipschitz constant of the network, contributing to improved generalization performance. Empirically, we validate DIPNet across a wide range of architectures and tasks, including Vision Transformers (ViTs), Large Language Models (LLMs), ResNet and MLPs. Our method consistently enhances test performance under standard settings, adversarial attacks, out-of-distribution inputs, and reasoning benchmarks. We demonstrate that the proposed input projection strategy can be seamlessly integrated into existing models, providing a general and effective approach for boosting generalization performance in modern deep learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Distributional Input Projection Networks (DIPNet), a novel framework for improving generalization in deep learning models. DIPNet projects inputs into learnable distributions at each layer, which implicitly promotes smoothness in the loss landscape. The authors provide theoretical analysis to show that DIPNet reduces smoothness measures and Lipschitz constants, contributing to improved generalization.  Empirical results across various architectures (ViTs, LLMs, ResNets, MLPs) and tasks (standard settings, adversarial attacks, out-of-distribution inputs, reasoning benchmarks) demonstrate consistent performance enhancements. The method is presented as a generally applicable technique for boosting generalization with minimal overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of projecting inputs into learnable distributions at each layer to enforce smoothness is interesting. It's a non-trivial approach to tackle the well-known challenge of promoting smoothness in deep networks without sacrificing performance. The authors appropriately contrast their method with adversarial training and random smoothing. However, injecting noise or perturbations into the input is a well-established concept, even at multiple layers (though not always learnable or integrated architecturally). The novelty lies in the particular way the learnable distribution is integrated, and how its parameters are regularized. Further, there appears to be theoretical guarantees for improved generalization performance.

*   **Significance:** Generalization is a fundamental problem in deep learning. A method that can consistently improve performance across a wide range of architectures and tasks is significant. The empirical results, while not revolutionary in magnitude, show consistent gains in several challenging scenarios, including adversarial robustness and OOD generalization, which are critical areas of research. The method's easy integration into existing architectures adds to its significance. The theoretical analysis, while not entirely groundbreaking, helps provide some justification for the observed empirical gains.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined method with theoretical justification.
    *   Extensive experimental validation across various architectures and tasks.
    *   Consistent improvements in challenging scenarios.
    *   Easy integration into existing models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty of the core idea, while not a simple rehash of existing techniques, could be argued to be an incremental improvement to existing noise injection and regularization strategies. Other methods accomplish input or hidden layer perturbations or regularization with different means.
    *   While consistent, the magnitude of improvement in some cases might be considered modest. The relative improvement is more interesting than the absolute.
    *   The theoretical analysis, while providing support, may not fully explain all the empirical phenomena observed.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by offering a practical and effective technique for improving generalization in deep learning. Its ease of integration and consistent performance gains may encourage its adoption by researchers and practitioners. The theoretical analysis could also inspire further research into distributional input representations and their impact on smoothness and generalization. The DIPNet idea or related methods can be easily tested and extended by other researchers.

*   **Critical Perspective:** The paper presents a solid and well-executed piece of research. The core idea is novel enough, and the empirical results are strong enough to warrant attention from the community. However, one should also be wary of a consistent trend for "architectural" solutions. At the same time, the authors explore several avenues and thoroughly document their methodology.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a meaningful contribution to the field of deep learning generalization. While the core idea builds on existing techniques, the specific implementation, theoretical justification, and extensive empirical validation make it a valuable addition to the literature. The method's easy integration and consistent performance gains, particularly in challenging scenarios, make it a potentially impactful contribution. A slightly higher score could be justified with larger magnitude improvements in certain evaluations, or if the theoretical analysis provided a more complete explanation of the method's behavior.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cracking the Code: Enhancing Implicit Hate Speech Detection through Coding Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04693v1)
- **Authors**: Lu Wei, Liangzhi Li, Tong Xiang, Xiao Liu, Noa Garcia
- **Abstract**: The internet has become a hotspot for hate speech (HS), threatening societal harmony and individual well-being. While automatic detection methods perform well in identifying explicit hate speech (ex-HS), they struggle with more subtle forms, such as implicit hate speech (im-HS). We tackle this problem by introducing a new taxonomy for im-HS detection, defining six encoding strategies named codetypes. We present two methods for integrating codetypes into im-HS detection: 1) prompting large language models (LLMs) directly to classify sentences based on generated responses, and 2) using LLMs as encoders with codetypes embedded during the encoding process. Experiments show that the use of codetypes improves im-HS detection in both Chinese and English datasets, validating the effectiveness of our approach across different languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Cracking the Code: Enhancing Implicit Hate Speech Detection through Coding Classification":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of detecting implicit hate speech (im-HS), which is more subtle and harder to identify than explicit hate speech (ex-HS). The authors propose a new taxonomy of six "codetypes" that represent different encoding strategies used in im-HS (Abbreviation, Metaphor, Irony, Pun, Idiom, Argot).  They present two methods to integrate these codetypes into im-HS detection: 1) Prompt-based classification using Large Language Models (LLMs), where LLMs are prompted to classify sentences based on the codetypes and content and 2) Embedding-based classification, where LLMs are used as frozen encoders with codetypes embedded during encoding.  The experiments, conducted on both Chinese and English datasets, demonstrate that incorporating codetypes improves im-HS detection accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel and potentially useful approach to im-HS detection by focusing on specific encoding strategies (codetypes). While the concept of rhetorical analysis isn't entirely new, the formalization of these strategies into a concrete taxonomy applicable to im-HS and the systematic integration of this taxonomy into LLM-based detection methods represent a valuable contribution. The cross-lingual validation adds to the novelty and robustness of the approach.

*   **Significance:** Hate speech detection is a critical task with significant societal implications.  The paper directly addresses the limitations of existing methods in handling the more challenging form of im-HS. By improving the detection of im-HS, the work has the potential to contribute to more effective content moderation and a safer online environment. The cross-lingual validation suggests a broader applicability of the method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The codetype taxonomy is well-defined, although derived empirically.
    *   The integration methods are clearly explained and relatively straightforward to implement.
    *   The use of both prompt-based and embedding-based methods provides a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   The evaluation on both Chinese and English datasets demonstrates the cross-lingual potential of the approach.
    *   The paper is well-written and structured.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The selection of codetypes, while presented as derived from observation, could benefit from a more rigorous theoretical grounding in linguistics or rhetoric.
    *   The improvement achieved by incorporating codetypes, while statistically significant, might not be transformative in absolute terms. The F1-scores reported are moderate in many cases, suggesting that the challenge of im-HS detection remains significant even with this approach.
    *   The taxonomy is somewhat limited in scope.  While the selected codetypes account for a significant portion of im-HS, other strategies may exist that are not captured.
    *   The reliance on LLMs introduces potential biases inherent in these models, which could impact the fairness and accuracy of the detection process.
    *   The "NOTE" at the beginning could detract and should be placed at the conclusion of the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging researchers to move beyond simple keyword-based or sentiment-based approaches to im-HS detection and to consider more nuanced linguistic and rhetorical features. The codetype taxonomy could serve as a useful framework for future research and development in this area.

* **Rigorous Rationale:**
    * The paper has merit in addressing the critical and growing issue of implicit hate speech, which is often harder to detect and thus presents significant challenges for content moderation. Its cross-lingual approach adds significant value, suggesting broad applicability.

    * However, its limitations are evident, particularly in the moderate performance improvement, its dependence on LLMs, and the potential for bias. These LLMs might inadvertently amplify existing biases or over-flag certain speech patterns, resulting in unintended censorship.

    * Additionally, the somewhat subjective nature of defining hate speech and its context further complicates the application of this method, potentially leading to errors in both detection and classification.
    * Given the complexities in its current state, a moderate score appears apt, acknowledging the paper's value but also emphasizing areas for essential future enhancements.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Empowering Economic Simulation for Massively Multiplayer Online Games through Generative Agent-Based Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04699v1)
- **Authors**: Bihan Xu, Shiwei Zhao, Runze Wu, Zhenya Huang, Jiawei Wang, Zhipeng Hu, Kai Wang, Haoyu Liu, Tangjie Lv, Le Li, Changjie Fan, Xin Tong, Jiangze Han
- **Abstract**: Within the domain of Massively Multiplayer Online (MMO) economy research, Agent-Based Modeling (ABM) has emerged as a robust tool for analyzing game economics, evolving from rule-based agents to decision-making agents enhanced by reinforcement learning. Nevertheless, existing works encounter significant challenges when attempting to emulate human-like economic activities among agents, particularly regarding agent reliability, sociability, and interpretability. In this study, we take a preliminary step in introducing a novel approach using Large Language Models (LLMs) in MMO economy simulation. Leveraging LLMs' role-playing proficiency, generative capacity, and reasoning aptitude, we design LLM-driven agents with human-like decision-making and adaptability. These agents are equipped with the abilities of role-playing, perception, memory, and reasoning, addressing the aforementioned challenges effectively. Simulation experiments focusing on in-game economic activities demonstrate that LLM-empowered agents can promote emergent phenomena like role specialization and price fluctuations in line with market rules.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to economic simulation in Massively Multiplayer Online Games (MMOs) by integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into Agent-Based Modeling (ABM).  It addresses limitations in traditional ABM (reliability, sociability, and interpretability) by creating LLM-driven agents (MMOAgents) equipped with role-playing, generative, and reasoning capabilities.  The MMOAgent framework comprises five modules: profile (data-driven player personalization), perception (game environment interpretation), reasoning (action determination), memory (experience logging), and action (game action execution). The paper demonstrates that these agents can exhibit human-like economic behaviors, fostering emergent phenomena like role specialization and realistic price fluctuations.  The system also facilitates player-to-player trading with linguistic negotiation and bargaining.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the explicit and thorough integration of LLMs within an ABM framework *specifically* tailored for MMO economic simulations. While LLMs have been used in multi-agent systems before, their application to simulate the complex dynamics of MMO economies, including player-to-player interaction and economic behaviors, appears to be a significant and novel contribution. Specifically, addressing the sociability component by integrating player communications like public and private chat for negotiation is a novel addition.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the potential to improve the realism and depth of MMO economic simulations. This, in turn, can empower game developers to design more robust and engaging economic policies. Improving the simulation reliability, sociability, and interpretability helps bridge the gap between abstract economic models and real-world gaming conditions. This creates a pathway for practical decision-making, a much-needed advancement.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Data-Driven Approach:**  The use of real-world player data to generate agent profiles improves the authenticity of the simulation.

    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The MMOAgent framework provides a well-defined structure for building LLM-driven agents. The modular design makes it extensible and potentially adaptable to other contexts.
    *   **Emphasis on Sociability:**  The inclusion of linguistic communication channels adds a crucial layer of realism.
    *   **Validation:**  The experimental results demonstrating role specialization and realistic price fluctuations are compelling. Ablation study and human evaluation help to analyze the module effectiveness.
    *   **Implementation details** The paper provides open-source implementation to help others to replicate and extend it.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM Hallucinations:** The paper acknowledges LLM hallucinations as a limitation, which might lead to agents performing nonsensical actions. This necessitates safeguards and might limit the simulation's fidelity.
    *   **Dependence on specific MMO:** The approach might be highly dependent on the architecture and rules of the target MMO. Broad applicability could be a question.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Running complex simulations with multiple LLM-driven agents can be computationally intensive and expensive. The work doesn't fully address the cost implications.
    *   **Limited Complexity:** Although the agents simulate economic activity, the range of actions available may still be less complex than a human player would employ.

*   **Potential Influence:**  This research could significantly influence the design and analysis of MMO economies. It also has potential implications for other fields that use agent-based modeling, especially those that require more realistic and socially intelligent agent behavior, such as market simulations, urban planning or even social simulations. The research also shows new possibilities to utilize powerful LLMs in complex virtual world simulations.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper demonstrates a strong combination of novelty and practical significance. Integrating LLMs within ABM for realistic MMO economic simulation and the human data driven approach is clearly and precisely novel. The inclusion of complex factors like player-to-player interaction and player personality in model is what drives the model to be more successful than traditional methods. While limitations exist related to LLM hallucinations and computational cost, the potential for impact on the MMO industry and broader agent-based modeling research warrants a high score. The weaknesses are acknowledged, and the framework provides a good foundation for future research to build upon and address these issues.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme for phoneme-based speech recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04711v1)
- **Authors**: Te Ma, Min Bi, Saierdaer Yusuyin, Hao Huang, Zhijian Ou
- **Abstract**: In automatic speech recognition (ASR), phoneme-based multilingual pre-training and crosslingual fine-tuning is attractive for its high data efficiency and competitive results compared to subword-based models. However, Weighted Finite State Transducer (WFST) based decoding is limited by its complex pipeline and inability to leverage large language models (LLMs). Therefore, we propose LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme (LLM-P2G) decoding for phoneme-based ASR, consisting of speech-to-phoneme (S2P) and phoneme-to-grapheme (P2G). A challenge is that there seems to have information loss in cascading S2P and P2G. To address this challenge, we propose two training strategies: data augmentation with noisy phonemes (DANP), and randomized top-$K$ marginalized (TKM) training and decoding. Our experimental results show that LLM-P2G outperforms WFST-based systems in crosslingual ASR for Polish and German, by relative WER reductions of 3.6% and 6.9% respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-P2G, an LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme decoding method for phoneme-based Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR). It aims to address the limitations of traditional WFST-based decoding, particularly its complex pipeline and inability to effectively leverage LLMs. The LLM-P2G approach consists of a speech-to-phoneme (S2P) model followed by a phoneme-to-grapheme (P2G) model implemented using an LLM. To combat potential information loss when cascading the S2P and P2G components, the authors propose two training strategies: data augmentation with noisy phonemes (DANP) and randomized top-K marginalized (TKM) training and decoding.  Experiments on crosslingual ASR tasks (Polish and German) demonstrate that LLM-P2G, especially when combined with DANP or TKM, can outperform WFST-based systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the application of LLMs for phoneme-to-grapheme conversion in phoneme-based ASR.  Prior work explored using LLMs for ASR error correction or through continuous embeddings, but the paper is unique in the direct phoneme-to-text conversion. The DANP and randomized TKM strategies are valuable contributions that address a critical challenge in cascaded ASR systems—the propagation of errors. The idea of marginalizing over top-K phoneme sequences is adapted from RAG techniques but creatively applied to the ASR context. However, prior work has investigated the use of LLMs in ASR, and the contribution is more of an engineering improvement than a fundamental conceptual leap.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to be significant because it provides a more streamlined alternative to WFST-based decoding in phoneme-based ASR. WFSTs have well-known complexities, and the LLM-P2G approach offers a simpler pipeline that can directly benefit from the language understanding and generation capabilities of LLMs. The observed improvements in crosslingual ASR tasks, especially the relative WER reductions for Polish and German, further highlight the potential of the method. The crosslingual aspect makes the research practically useful since it focuses on low-resource settings. This is crucial because the impact on low-resource speech recognition can be a differentiator.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined approach with two distinct training strategies.
    *   Experimental results demonstrating improvements over WFST-based baselines.
    *   Detailed ablation studies providing insights into the effectiveness of DANP and TKM.
    *   Reproducible results, code, and model provided, which will facilitate wider research and adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The performance of LLM-P2G is heavily reliant on the pre-training data of the LLM. The paper acknowledges that the limited pre-training data for Polish affects the results in low-resource scenarios.
    *   The experiments are limited to only two languages.  While these provide a valuable starting point, more extensive evaluation across a broader range of languages is needed to assess the generalizability of the approach.
    *   The paper does not fully investigate computational complexity. The LLM-P2G approach, especially with TKM, could be computationally expensive compared to WFST-based decoding. This aspect needs to be examined.
    *  The paper relies solely on CommonVoice data, which is known for its specific characteristics (e.g., read speech). Testing on more varied and challenging datasets (e.g., conversational speech, noisy environments) would strengthen the claims.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach could influence future research in phoneme-based ASR by popularizing the use of LLMs for decoding. The proposed DANP and TKM training strategies may be adopted in other cascaded ASR systems, not just with LLMs. Furthermore, the simplification of the ASR pipeline may encourage the exploration of new architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to phoneme-based ASR by integrating LLMs into the decoding process. The reported performance improvements, especially when using the TKM strategy, are significant.  The crosslingual setting emphasizes the applicability of the method in low-resource scenarios. However, the paper has limitations, including its reliance on LLM pre-training data and the limited number of languages tested.  Also, the fundamental architectural shift is relatively small. Given these factors, a score of 7 reflects the novelty, significance, and practical utility of the LLM-P2G, while acknowledging the weaknesses in its generalizability and computational cost.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Holistic Visual Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Videos: A LLM-Based Multi-Dimensional Evaluation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04715v1)
- **Authors**: Zelu Qi, Ping Shi, Chaoyang Zhang, Shuqi Wang, Fei Zhao, Da Pan, Zefeng Ying
- **Abstract**: The development of AI-Generated Video (AIGV) technology has been remarkable in recent years, significantly transforming the paradigm of video content production. However, AIGVs still suffer from noticeable visual quality defects, such as noise, blurriness, frame jitter and low dynamic degree, which severely impact the user's viewing experience. Therefore, an effective automatic visual quality assessment is of great importance for AIGV content regulation and generative model improvement. In this work, we decompose the visual quality of AIGVs into three dimensions: technical quality, motion quality, and video semantics. For each dimension, we design corresponding encoder to achieve effective feature representation. Moreover, considering the outstanding performance of large language models (LLMs) in various vision and language tasks, we introduce a LLM as the quality regression module. To better enable the LLM to establish reasoning associations between multi-dimensional features and visual quality, we propose a specially designed multi-modal prompt engineering framework. Additionally, we incorporate LoRA fine-tuning technology during the training phase, allowing the LLM to better adapt to specific tasks. Our proposed method achieved \textbf{second place} in the NTIRE 2025 Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Content Challenge: Track 2 AI Generated video, demonstrating its effectiveness. Codes can be obtained at https://github.com/QiZelu/AIGVEval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AIGVEval, a novel framework for assessing the visual quality of AI-generated videos (AIGVs).  It decomposes visual quality into three dimensions: technical quality (blurriness, noise), motion quality (dynamic degree, frame jitter), and video semantics (color, texture, scene description). Each dimension is processed by a dedicated encoder (3D Swin Transformer for technical quality, SlowFast-R50 for motion, and BLIP for semantics). The encoded features are then fed into a large language model (LLM), Vicuna v1.5, for quality regression. To improve the LLM's understanding and reasoning capabilities, the authors propose a multi-modal prompt engineering framework with semantic anchors. They also employ LoRA fine-tuning to adapt the LLM to the specific task of AIGV quality evaluation.  The method achieved second place in the NTIRE 2025 Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Content Challenge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable level of novelty, but it doesn't revolutionize the field. The decomposition of video quality into three dimensions is conceptually sound, but it's not entirely new. The use of specific encoders (3D Swin, SlowFast, BLIP) for each dimension is a good engineering choice based on existing work, rather than a fundamental innovation. The key novelty lies in the LLM-based quality regression module and the proposed prompt engineering with semantic anchors. While LLMs have been used for video quality assessment before, the specific prompt structure aimed at guiding the LLM's reasoning based on the explicit separation of features *is* a valuable and relatively unexplored area. Integrating LoRA fine-tuning is a standard, but practical, way to adapt the LLM.

*   **Significance:** The work contributes to the growing field of AI-generated content evaluation.  Accurately assessing AIGV quality is crucial for regulating content and improving generative models. The AIGVEval framework offers a structured approach, and the focus on interpretable features (through the dimensional decomposition) is a positive aspect. The prompt engineering design is particularly helpful, and shows a good way to interpret features with the LLM for better result. The experimental results, especially the second-place finish in the NTIRE challenge, demonstrate the practical effectiveness of the method. However, the gains over existing methods are not dramatically large, which limits the overall significance. Also, while the NTIRE challenge result is encouraging, further analysis of the model's performance across different types of AIGVs and distortion patterns would strengthen the claims. The paper also lacks a detailed explanation of how the weights for combining the LLM scores are determined (equation 5).

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Logical framework with a modular design.
    *   Effective use of existing pre-trained models.
    *   Novel prompt engineering strategy to enhance LLM reasoning.
    *   Solid experimental results, including a successful challenge entry.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental improvements rather than breakthrough innovation.
    *   The novelty is limited to the prompt engineering and integration of components, not in any individual encoder or the dimensionality decomposition.
    *   Limited analysis of the model's strengths and weaknesses across various AIGV types.
    *   The performance difference between the model with and without LoRA fine-tuning is not provided in the ablation study, so it is not easy to conclude the benefits of LoRA fine-tuning.
    *   Not explained how the weights for combining the LLM scores are determined.
    *   The performance is not high on the open-source datatset T2VQA-DB, but it is also not considered its consistency.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:** The paper presents a sound and practical framework for AIGV quality assessment. The dimensional decomposition and prompt engineering are valuable contributions that address a specific weakness in previous LLM-based approaches. The experimental results provide evidence of effectiveness. However, the paper lacks a major breakthrough and relies heavily on existing models and techniques. While the integration is well-executed, the overall novelty and potential impact are moderate. A deeper analysis of the model's behavior and a more thorough exploration of different AIGV scenarios would have strengthened the paper. However, its innovative prompt engineering and overall performance do warrant a score above average.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Learning dissection trajectories from expert surgical videos via imitation learning with equivariant diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04716v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyu Wang, Yonghao Long, Yueyao Chen, Hon-Chi Yip, Markus Scheppach, Philip Wai-Yan Chiu, Yeung Yam, Helen Mei-Ling Meng, Qi Dou
- **Abstract**: Endoscopic Submucosal Dissection (ESD) is a well-established technique for removing epithelial lesions. Predicting dissection trajectories in ESD videos offers significant potential for enhancing surgical skill training and simplifying the learning process, yet this area remains underexplored. While imitation learning has shown promise in acquiring skills from expert demonstrations, challenges persist in handling uncertain future movements, learning geometric symmetries, and generalizing to diverse surgical scenarios. To address these, we introduce a novel approach: Implicit Diffusion Policy with Equivariant Representations for Imitation Learning (iDPOE). Our method models expert behavior through a joint state action distribution, capturing the stochastic nature of dissection trajectories and enabling robust visual representation learning across various endoscopic views. By incorporating a diffusion model into policy learning, iDPOE ensures efficient training and sampling, leading to more accurate predictions and better generalization. Additionally, we enhance the model's ability to generalize to geometric symmetries by embedding equivariance into the learning process. To address state mismatches, we develop a forward-process guided action inference strategy for conditional sampling. Using an ESD video dataset of nearly 2000 clips, experimental results show that our approach surpasses state-of-the-art methods, both explicit and implicit, in trajectory prediction. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first application of imitation learning to surgical skill development for dissection trajectory prediction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Learning Dissection Trajectories from Expert Surgical Videos via Imitation Learning with Equivariant Diffusion":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel imitation learning approach, termed Implicit Diffusion Policy with Equivariant Representations (iDPOE), for predicting dissection trajectories in endoscopic submucosal dissection (ESD) surgical videos.  The core idea is to learn an implicit policy using a diffusion model coupled with equivariant representations to handle the stochastic nature of surgical movements, geometric symmetries, and diverse surgical scenarios. The diffusion model learns a joint state-action distribution, while equivariant representations improve generalization by explicitly modeling rotational symmetry. A forward-diffusion guided action inference strategy addresses state mismatches during conditional sampling.  Experiments on a collected ESD video dataset demonstrate that iDPOE outperforms existing trajectory prediction methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of implicit diffusion policies and equivariant representations for surgical trajectory prediction is reasonably novel. While diffusion models and equivariant representations are individually established techniques, their specific application in the context of surgical skill learning from videos and their fusion to handle the specific challenges of surgical trajectory prediction represent a genuine advance. The forward-diffusion guidance strategy also adds a unique element, addressing a specific problem encountered during inference with implicit models.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem: assisting surgeons with dissection trajectory planning in ESD procedures. Accurate trajectory prediction could indeed enhance surgical training, reduce errors, and improve patient outcomes.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained methodology, with a good balance between theoretical background and implementation details.
    *   Demonstrated improvement over existing methods in the experiments.
    *   Comprehensive experiments, including "in-the-context" and "out-of-the-context" scenarios, ablation studies, and evaluation of performance under blurred visual conditions, which provide strong evidence for the robustness of the approach.
    *   Discussion of computational complexity.
    *   Clear and concise writing.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dataset is from a single institution and a single surgeon's experience. This might limit generalizability to different surgical techniques, patient anatomies, or equipment.
    *   The evaluation focuses on quantitative metrics. While ADE, FDE, and FD are standard, qualitative assessments (e.g., surgeon evaluation of predicted trajectories) would further strengthen the claims of clinical relevance.
    *   While the paper mentions the potential for synthetic data generation, the evaluation of the generated data is somewhat superficial and doesn't thoroughly explore its utility in realistic downstream tasks.
    *   The implementation of equivariance using only cyclic group C4 might be a simplification and might not fully capture all possible rotational symmetries in the surgical scene.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research in surgical skill learning, particularly in areas like trajectory prediction and surgical assistance systems. The use of diffusion models with equivariant representations could become a standard approach in the field.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of surgical skill learning.  The combination of implicit diffusion models and equivariant representations is a well-motivated and technically sound approach to a challenging problem. The experimental results support the claims of improved performance and robustness. However, the limitations regarding dataset diversity, lack of in-depth evaluation of synthetic data and the simplified treatment of rotational symmetries slightly detract from the overall impact. Given these considerations, a score of 7 is warranted.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Lifelong Evolution: Collaborative Learning between Large and Small Language Models for Continuous Emergent Fake News Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04739v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyi Zhou, Xiaoming Zhang, Litian Zhang, Yibo Zhang, Zhenyu Guan, Chaozhuo Li, Philip S. Yu
- **Abstract**: The widespread dissemination of fake news on social media has significantly impacted society, resulting in serious consequences. Conventional deep learning methodologies employing small language models (SLMs) suffer from extensive supervised training requirements and difficulties adapting to evolving news environments due to data scarcity and distribution shifts. Large language models (LLMs), despite robust zero-shot capabilities, fall short in accurately detecting fake news owing to outdated knowledge and the absence of suitable demonstrations. In this paper, we propose a novel Continuous Collaborative Emergent Fake News Detection (C$^2$EFND) framework to address these challenges. The C$^2$EFND framework strategically leverages both LLMs' generalization power and SLMs' classification expertise via a multi-round collaborative learning framework. We further introduce a lifelong knowledge editing module based on a Mixture-of-Experts architecture to incrementally update LLMs and a replay-based continue learning method to ensure SLMs retain prior knowledge without retraining entirely. Extensive experiments on Pheme and Twitter16 datasets demonstrate that C$^2$EFND significantly outperforms existed methods, effectively improving detection accuracy and adaptability in continuous emergent fake news scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel framework called Continuous Collaborative Emergent Fake News Detection (C2EFND) for detecting fake news in a dynamic, evolving environment. C2EFND leverages both large language models (LLMs) for their generalization abilities and small language models (SLMs) for their classification expertise.  The framework employs a multi-round collaborative learning process, a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) based lifelong knowledge editing module for LLMs, and a replay-based continual learning method for SLMs.  Experiments on Pheme and Twitter16 datasets demonstrate that C2EFND outperforms existing methods in terms of accuracy and adaptability to continuous emergent fake news scenarios. The core ideas involve actively selecting informative samples, continuously updating the knowledge of both LLMs and SLMs, and facilitating collaborative learning between them.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components, including:

*   **C2EFND Framework:** The overall architecture combining active learning, lifelong knowledge editing (MoE for LLMs), continual learning (replay for SLMs), and multi-round collaborative learning is a significant contribution. While each component has roots in existing literature, their combination and application to the specific problem of emergent fake news detection is novel.
*   **MoE-based Lifelong Knowledge Editing:** The use of a Mixture-of-Experts approach for incrementally updating LLMs' knowledge is a practical and efficient way to adapt to new events without full retraining.
*   **Two-Stage Active Learning:** The approach to active learning, combining diversity-based and uncertainty-based sampling, represents an improvement over standard active learning strategies in this context. This is particularly useful given the distribution shifts inherent in the problem of emergent news.
*   **Collaborative Multi-round learning:** Iteratively refining model predictions by exploiting semi-supervised learning strategies to improve both the data quality and the training of the model.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant and timely problem: the spread of fake news in dynamic environments. Current fake news detection methods often struggle to adapt to new events due to data scarcity and distribution shifts. The proposed framework offers a promising solution by integrating the strengths of LLMs and SLMs. The experimental results demonstrate that C2EFND achieves state-of-the-art performance on real-world datasets, suggesting that it could have a practical impact on fake news detection systems.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Approach:** C2EFND tackles the key challenges of emergent fake news detection, including data scarcity, continuous knowledge updating, and effective collaboration between models.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides convincing experimental evidence that C2EFND outperforms existing methods on two benchmark datasets.
*   **Well-Designed Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.
*   **Clear and Well-Organized:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the proposed method and experimental results.
*   **Addresses an Important Problem:** Fake news detection is critical in mitigating the negative societal impacts of misinformation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** While the MoE approach reduces the cost of retraining, the overall framework might still be computationally expensive due to the multiple models (LLM and SLM) and the iterative learning process. The paper could benefit from a discussion on the computational efficiency of C2EFND.
*   **Reliance on LLM Quality:** The performance of C2EFND depends on the quality of the LLM and its ability to generate accurate rationales and knowledge. If the LLM is biased or unreliable, the performance of the entire framework could be affected. Further robustness studies could be beneficial.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The hyperparameter sensitivity analysis shows some variability in performance based on parameter choices. In particular, there appears to be a rather narrow sweet spot for many of the hyperparameters. Providing more guidance for selecting the optimal hyperparameter values would be helpful.
*   **Limited Discussion on Deployment Challenges:** While the paper focuses on improving accuracy, it could provide more discussion on the practical challenges of deploying C2EFND in real-world scenarios, such as scalability, latency, and robustness to adversarial attacks.
*   **Dataset Limitations**: While Pheme and Twitter16 are standard datasets, they are relatively small. A more comprehensive evaluation on larger and more diverse datasets could strengthen the paper's claims.

**Potential Influence:** C2EFND has the potential to influence the development of more accurate and adaptable fake news detection systems. The proposed framework could also inspire new research directions in continual learning, knowledge editing, and collaborative learning for other NLP tasks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** C2EFND presents a novel and well-executed framework for a very important problem. It contains innovative components like the MoE-based lifelong knowledge editing and two-stage active learning, which contribute to significant performance improvements. However, the paper could be strengthened by addressing some of the weaknesses mentioned above, such as computational cost, sensitivity to the underlying LLM, dataset limitations, deployment challenges and hyperparameter sensitivity. While the algorithmic ideas are strong, the score is not higher due to those limitations and a general lack of extremely surprising or paradigm-shifting insights. Nevertheless, the paper is a strong contribution to the field and warrants publication.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multi-Layer GRPO: Enhancing Reasoning and Self-Correction in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04746v1)
- **Authors**: Fei Ding, Baiqiao Wang, Zijian Zeng, Youwei Wang
- **Abstract**: The Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm has demonstrated considerable success in enhancing the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs), as evidenced by DeepSeek-R1. However, the absence of intermediate supervision in GRPO frequently leads to inefficient exploration dynamics. A single error in a complex reasoning chain can invalidate the entire solution, resulting in abrupt reward vanishing and compromising training stability.To address these challenges, we propose MGRPO (Multi-layer GRPO). MGRPO operates in two layers: the first layer employs standard GRPO to generate an initial response. This response, along with the original query, is then fed into a second-layer GRPO process. This second layer is specifically trained to identify and correct errors in the initial response, effectively creating a self-correction loop. This mechanism provides implicit process-level supervision by rewarding successful error correction, without requiring an explicit, densely-annotated reward model. Experimental results on several mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that MGRPO significantly outperforms standard GRPO, achieving superior performance by fostering both reasoning and self-correction abilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Multi-Layer GRPO (MGRPO), an extension of the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm designed to improve reasoning and self-correction in large language models (LLMs). MGRPO employs a two-layer GRPO process: the first layer generates an initial response, and the second layer focuses on identifying and correcting errors in the first layer's output.  This self-correction loop is implicitly supervised through outcome-based rewards, removing the need for explicit dense reward models.  The authors present a correction-augmentation-selection mechanism for the second layer and evaluate MGRPO on mathematical reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating performance improvements over standard GRPO and other baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using a second layer to explicitly focus on self-correction is a valuable contribution.  Building on the GRPO framework and introducing a second, refinement layer provides a structured way to improve LLM reasoning. The idea of implicit process supervision through the self-correction layer is also a nice twist. However, self-correction through prompting or reinforcement learning is not entirely new (citing SCORe and other self-correction methods), though MGRPO presents a specific architecture and training paradigm tailored to GRPO.
