### SCOP: Evaluating the Comprehension Process of Large Language Models from a Cognitive View
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05000v1)
- **Authors**: Yongjie Xiao, Hongru Liang, Peixin Qin, Yao Zhang, Wenqiang Lei
- **Abstract**: Despite the great potential of large language models(LLMs) in machine comprehension, it is still disturbing to fully count on them in real-world scenarios. This is probably because there is no rational explanation for whether the comprehension process of LLMs is aligned with that of experts. In this paper, we propose SCOP to carefully examine how LLMs perform during the comprehension process from a cognitive view. Specifically, it is equipped with a systematical definition of five requisite skills during the comprehension process, a strict framework to construct testing data for these skills, and a detailed analysis of advanced open-sourced and closed-sourced LLMs using the testing data. With SCOP, we find that it is still challenging for LLMs to perform an expert-level comprehension process. Even so, we notice that LLMs share some similarities with experts, e.g., performing better at comprehending local information than global information. Further analysis reveals that LLMs can be somewhat unreliable -- they might reach correct answers through flawed comprehension processes. Based on SCOP, we suggest that one direction for improving LLMs is to focus more on the comprehension process, ensuring all comprehension skills are thoroughly developed during training.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SCOP: Evaluating the Comprehension Process of Large Language Models from a Cognitive View":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SCOP, a new framework for evaluating the comprehension process of large language models (LLMs) from a cognitive perspective. It argues that current LLM evaluations primarily focus on answer correctness, neglecting the underlying comprehension process. SCOP addresses this by:

1.  **Defining requisite comprehension skills:** It breaks down comprehension into five skills: locating, inferring, connecting, organizing, and selecting. These are grounded in cognitive theories.
2.  **Providing a data construction framework:** It outlines a strict methodology for creating testing data tailored to each of the five skills, covering both narrative and expository document types. The datasets are constructed from existing resources and new data crawls, and are rigorously filtered to remove questions answerable from memory or other "shortcuts".
3.  **Conducting detailed analysis of LLMs:** The paper evaluates several open-source and closed-source LLMs on the SCOP benchmark, analyzing their performance across the different skills, document types, and answer styles.

The authors find that LLMs still fall short of expert-level comprehension and exhibit inconsistent behaviors, sometimes arriving at the correct answers through flawed processes. They observe that LLMs generally perform better on local comprehension (locating skills) compared to global comprehension (inferring and interpreting skills). They also find variations in performance among different LLMs, suggesting discrepancies between comprehension process evaluation and answer-based evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its cognitive-inspired approach to evaluating LLM comprehension.  Shifting the focus from solely outcome-based metrics to analyzing the process is a valuable contribution. The breakdown into distinct comprehension skills is useful for identifying specific areas where LLMs struggle. The data construction framework, with its emphasis on filtering out memory-based answers, is a solid and important contribution for establishing a rigorous evaluation. While there are prior works on evaluating specific linguistic capabilities of LLMs (e.g., coreference resolution), SCOP offers a more holistic and integrated evaluation of comprehension.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation. As LLMs are increasingly deployed in real-world, safety-critical applications, understanding how they arrive at their answers becomes paramount. Simply measuring accuracy is insufficient. SCOP provides a methodology and a benchmark for scrutinizing the internal comprehension mechanisms of LLMs, potentially leading to improved training strategies that foster genuine understanding rather than shortcut-based performance.  The observation that LLMs can exhibit "inconsistent behavior" – correct answers with flawed reasoning – is a crucial finding, emphasizing the need for explainable AI and a cautious approach to deploying LLMs in high-stakes scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Cognitively Motivated Framework:** The breakdown of comprehension into skills has solid theoretical underpinnings.
    *   **Rigorous Data Construction:** The paper details a careful data curation process to control for potential biases and confounding factors.  The filtering methods for memorized data are particularly important.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The evaluation covers multiple LLMs and analyzes performance across different dimensions (skills, document types, answer styles).
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow. The motivations and findings are clearly articulated.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Skill Definition Complexity:** The boundaries between some of the comprehension skills (particularly inferring and interpreting) may be somewhat fuzzy.  Refining and clarifying these definitions further could strengthen the framework.
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The framework currently focuses on a specific set of comprehension skills, but it does not address the role of LLM's "world knowledge" which is often incorporated to provide the response. This may influence the assessment of the comprehension process.
    *   **Prompt Sensitivity:** While the paper acknowledges prompt sensitivity, a more detailed analysis of how different prompts might affect performance on the SCOP benchmark would be beneficial.
    *   **Over-reliance on Llama3-8B-Instruct for Data Construction:** The data generation pipelines relies Llama3-8B-Instruct, which may induce bias.

*   **Potential Influence:**  SCOP has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

    *   **Guiding Training Strategies:**  It can inform the development of training objectives and architectures that promote genuine comprehension.
    *   **Developing More Explainable LLMs:** It can encourage research on methods for making LLM reasoning more transparent.
    *   **Creating More Robust Evaluation Metrics:** It can inspire the creation of new evaluation metrics that go beyond answer correctness.
    *   **Enabling Safer LLM Deployment:** It promotes a more cautious approach to deploying LLMs in safety-critical domains.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and novel approach to evaluating LLM comprehension.  The cognitive motivation, rigorous methodology, and insightful findings are all significant contributions. The shift in focus from just correct answers to the evaluation of the comprehension process itself is crucial for understanding and improving LLMs. While the limitations outlined above exist, they do not significantly detract from the overall value of the work.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### QiMeng: Fully Automated Hardware and Software Design for Processor Chip
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05007v1)
- **Authors**: Rui Zhang, Yuanbo Wen, Shuyao Cheng, Di Huang, Shaohui Peng, Jiaming Guo, Pengwei Jin, Jiacheng Zhao, Tianrui Ma, Yaoyu Zhu, Yifan Hao, Yongwei Zhao, Shengwen Liang, Ying Wang, Xing Hu, Zidong Du, Huimin Cui, Ling Li, Qi Guo, Yunji Chen
- **Abstract**: Processor chip design technology serves as a key frontier driving breakthroughs in computer science and related fields. With the rapid advancement of information technology, conventional design paradigms face three major challenges: the physical constraints of fabrication technologies, the escalating demands for design resources, and the increasing diversity of ecosystems. Automated processor chip design has emerged as a transformative solution to address these challenges. While recent breakthroughs in Artificial Intelligence (AI), particularly Large Language Models (LLMs) techniques, have opened new possibilities for fully automated processor chip design, substantial challenges remain in establishing domain-specific LLMs for processor chip design. In this paper, we propose QiMeng, a novel system for fully automated hardware and software design of processor chips. QiMeng comprises three hierarchical layers. In the bottom-layer, we construct a domain-specific Large Processor Chip Model (LPCM) that introduces novel designs in architecture, training, and inference, to address key challenges such as knowledge representation gap, data scarcity, correctness assurance, and enormous solution space. In the middle-layer, leveraging the LPCM's knowledge representation and inference capabilities, we develop the Hardware Design Agent and the Software Design Agent to automate the design of hardware and software for processor chips. Currently, several components of QiMeng have been completed and successfully applied in various top-layer applications, demonstrating significant advantages and providing a feasible solution for efficient, fully automated hardware/software design of processor chips. Future research will focus on integrating all components and performing iterative top-down and bottom-up design processes to establish a comprehensive QiMeng system.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a rigorous evaluation of the paper "QiMeng: Fully Automated Hardware and Software Design for Processor Chip."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QiMeng, a novel system designed for the fully automated hardware and software design of processor chips. QiMeng addresses the growing challenges of conventional design paradigms including physical constraints, design resource limitations, and the increasing diversity of ecosystems. It leverages recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) to create a domain-specific Large Processor Chip Model (LPCM).  The system comprises three hierarchical layers: the LPCM at the bottom layer, Hardware and Software Design Agents in the middle layer that utilize the LPCM, and various top-layer processor chip design applications.  LPCM introduces novel designs in architecture, training, and inference to address knowledge representation, data scarcity, correctness assurance, and the large solution space. The Hardware and Software Design Agents automate the design process. The authors demonstrate significant advantages of the system by applying it to different top-layer applications. The paper outlines a roadmap for fully integrating all components and establishing a comprehensive, self-evolving QiMeng system.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The idea of using LLMs for processor chip design is certainly gaining traction, but QiMeng distinguishes itself through a few key aspects that add novelty.  First, the explicit design and construction of a *domain-specific* LPCM is significant.  This moves beyond simply applying off-the-shelf LLMs, and aims to tailor the model's architecture (multimodal), training (cross-stage collaborative), and inference (feedback-driven) specifically for the complex challenges of processor design. The dual-loop mechanisms implemented in the Hardware and Software Design Agents are also novel, combining performance optimization and automated verification/repair. These ensure correctness and performance. The iterative top-down and bottom-up approach with a "self-evolving" aspiration is conceptually intriguing, aiming for continuous improvement through integrated processes.

**Significance:**

The potential significance of this work is high. Processor chip design is a critical area, and automating the process could lead to faster development cycles, reduced costs, and more specialized architectures.  If QiMeng can deliver on its promise of surpassing manual design under identical fabrication technology, it would represent a paradigm shift. The ability to generate both hardware *and* software, coupled through design agents, is another strength, addressing the co-design requirement often overlooked in existing approaches.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive System Design:** QiMeng is not just a single tool but a system-level approach, addressing multiple aspects of chip design, from functional specifications to physical layouts and software integration.
*   **Domain-Specific LLM:** The emphasis on a specialized LPCM tackles the knowledge representation gap, data scarcity, and correctness challenges more effectively than generic LLMs.
*   **Feedback-Driven Approach:** The integration of functional correctness and performance feedback loops into the design agents' inference mechanism enhances the reliability and efficiency of the automated design process.
*   **Practical Applications:** The successful application of QiMeng components in various top-layer applications showcases the potential for real-world impact.
*   **Clear Roadmap:** The phased roadmap provides a clear direction for future research and development, highlighting the challenges and milestones along the way.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Implementation Details:** While the paper presents a detailed system design, specific implementation details of the LPCM, the design agents, and the feedback mechanisms are somewhat limited. More concrete examples and quantitative results would strengthen the claims.
*   **Lack of Complete Integration:** The paper states that several components have been completed and applied in various top-layer applications, but the complete end-to-end integrated system is still a work in progress. Demonstrating the performance of the *fully* integrated QiMeng system is crucial for validating its overall effectiveness. The system is "still in the first phase".
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** Some of the evaluation, particularly around the automated front-end design, compares against older processors (e.g., Intel 486). Comparing against more modern, state-of-the-art designs would provide a stronger argument for the system's capabilities.
*   **Enormous Scale Challenges:**  The stated dimensionality of the solution space (10^10540 for a 32-bit CPU) emphasizes the sheer complexity of the problem.  While the hierarchical approach helps, it's difficult to assess the extent to which QiMeng truly reduces the search space without significantly pruning potentially optimal solutions.

**Potential Influence:**

Despite its weaknesses, QiMeng has the potential to influence the field by providing a holistic framework for automated processor chip design. It draws from various fields of study and combines them into a single, self-improving framework. The design of LPCM and design agents that focus on performance and functional accuracy will be of interest to other researchers exploring AI and EDA.  The roadmap and ongoing experiments will offer more definitive analyses for improvements to the existing work.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6.5**. While the paper presents a compelling system design with several novel elements, the limited implementation details, lack of a fully integrated system, and comparison to outdated metrics detract from its overall impact *at this stage*. The core ideas are solid and potentially transformative, however, more concrete results and a demonstration of the integrated system are necessary to justify a higher score. The focus on LPCM is a very valuable addition.

- **Classification**: cs.AR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Automatic Robustness Stress Testing of LLMs as Mathematical Problem Solvers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05038v1)
- **Authors**: Yutao Hou, Zeguan Xiao, Fei Yu, Yihan Jiang, Xuetao Wei, Hailiang Huang, Yun Chen, Guanhua Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have achieved distinguished performance on various reasoning-intensive tasks. However, LLMs might still face the challenges of robustness issues and fail unexpectedly in some simple reasoning tasks. Previous works evaluate the LLM robustness with hand-crafted templates or a limited set of perturbation rules, indicating potential data contamination in pre-training or fine-tuning datasets. In this work, inspired by stress testing in software engineering, we propose a novel framework, Automatic Robustness Checker (AR-Checker), to generate mathematical problem variants that maintain the semantic meanings of the original one but might fail the LLMs. The AR-Checker framework generates mathematical problem variants through multi-round parallel streams of LLM-based rewriting and verification. Our framework can generate benchmark variants dynamically for each LLM, thus minimizing the risk of data contamination. Experiments on GSM8K and MATH-500 demonstrate the strong performance of AR-Checker on mathematical tasks. We also evaluate AR-Checker on benchmarks beyond mathematics, including MMLU, MMLU-Pro, and CommonsenseQA, where it also achieves strong performance, further proving the effectiveness of AR-Checker.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AR-CHECKER, a novel framework for automatically stress-testing Large Language Models (LLMs) as mathematical problem solvers. AR-CHECKER uses LLMs to rewrite mathematical problems into variants while preserving the original meaning. A verifier LLM ensures semantic consistency, and the target LLM attempts to solve the rewritten problem. The framework uses a multi-round iterative approach with parallel streams to generate diverse and challenging variants. Experiments on GSM8K and MATH-500 demonstrate that AR-CHECKER effectively identifies robustness issues in LLMs, often causing significant accuracy drops compared to the original benchmarks. The paper also shows its applicability on non-mathematical datasets like MMLU and CommonsenseQA, and investigates the influence of rewriter models, rewriting principles, and transferability of failed test cases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its automated, LLM-driven approach to robustness testing.  Existing methods often rely on hand-crafted templates or fixed perturbation rules. AR-CHECKER offers a more dynamic and adaptive approach by leveraging the reasoning and creative capabilities of LLMs themselves to generate adversarial examples.  The idea of framing robustness evaluation as a stress-testing problem, inspired by software engineering, is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial issue in the LLM field: the lack of reliable robustness evaluation.  Despite impressive performance on standard benchmarks, LLMs can still fail unexpectedly on seemingly simple tasks. AR-CHECKER provides a method to expose these vulnerabilities, offering insights into the weaknesses of LLMs and the limitations of existing evaluation metrics. The identified weakness types (e.g., breakdown in sequential reasoning, over-sensitivity to numerical variations) offer actionable insights for improving LLM design and training. By creating a system to minimize data contamination via dynamic benchmark creation, the authors make a solid attempt to improve evaluation methodology. The generalizability of the framework beyond mathematics is also a significant strength.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Automated and Adaptive:** AR-CHECKER eliminates the need for manual crafting of adversarial examples.
    *   **Dynamic Benchmark Creation:** Minimizes the risk of data contamination.
    *   **Actionable Insights:**  Provides insights into specific LLM weaknesses.
    *   **Generalizability:** Applicable to various domains beyond mathematics.
    *   **Scalability:** Demonstrates improved effectiveness with more powerful rewriter LLMs.
    *   **Rigorous Experiments:** Includes ablation studies, comparisons with existing benchmarks, and transferability analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Dependency:**  The framework's effectiveness relies heavily on the quality of the rewriter and verifier LLMs.  While the paper explores different rewriter models, it's possible that AR-CHECKER's limitations are tied to the capabilities of the chosen LLMs. Performance is likely tied to the quality and cost of LLM-based APIs.
    *   **Cost of Execution:** The LLM calls required for the rewriter and verifier introduce a significant computational cost, making it more expensive than static benchmarks.
    *   **Implicit Bias:** While aiming to minimize data contamination, there could be implicit biases in the rewriter and verifier LLMs that influence the types of variants generated and the assessment of core meaning.
    *   **Qualitative Evaluation:** While the quantitative results are solid, the weakness analysis could benefit from more qualitative exploration and deeper understanding of why specific rewrites cause failures.

*   **Potential Influence:** AR-CHECKER has the potential to influence the LLM research community by providing a new and more robust evaluation methodology. It could encourage the development of more robust LLMs and inspire further research on automated adversarial example generation. However, the computational cost may limit its widespread adoption. The findings related to transferability of weaknesses can guide the development of more model-specific defense strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

AR-CHECKER presents a significant advancement in LLM robustness evaluation. While there are limitations related to its reliance on LLMs and computational cost, the paper's novel approach, actionable insights, and solid experimental results justify a strong positive rating. The automation offered by AR-CHECKER makes it a scalable methodology for stress-testing LLMs in a dynamic fashion.

### FlowDirector: Training-Free Flow Steering for Precise Text-to-Video Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05046v1)
- **Authors**: Guangzhao Li, Yanming Yang, Chenxi Song, Chi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Text-driven video editing aims to modify video content according to natural language instructions. While recent training-free approaches have made progress by leveraging pre-trained diffusion models, they typically rely on inversion-based techniques that map input videos into the latent space, which often leads to temporal inconsistencies and degraded structural fidelity. To address this, we propose FlowDirector, a novel inversion-free video editing framework. Our framework models the editing process as a direct evolution in data space, guiding the video via an Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE) to smoothly transition along its inherent spatiotemporal manifold, thereby preserving temporal coherence and structural details. To achieve localized and controllable edits, we introduce an attention-guided masking mechanism that modulates the ODE velocity field, preserving non-target regions both spatially and temporally. Furthermore, to address incomplete edits and enhance semantic alignment with editing instructions, we present a guidance-enhanced editing strategy inspired by Classifier-Free Guidance, which leverages differential signals between multiple candidate flows to steer the editing trajectory toward stronger semantic alignment without compromising structural consistency. Extensive experiments across benchmarks demonstrate that FlowDirector achieves state-of-the-art performance in instruction adherence, temporal consistency, and background preservation, establishing a new paradigm for efficient and coherent video editing without inversion.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FlowDirector paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FlowDirector, a novel training-free framework for text-driven video editing. Unlike existing methods that rely on inversion techniques (mapping the video to a latent space), FlowDirector directly evolves the video in the data space using an Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE). This approach is claimed to better preserve temporal coherence and structural fidelity. The framework incorporates two key components: Spatially Attentive Flow Correction (SAFC) to ensure localized edits and prevent unintended changes in non-target regions, and Differential Averaging Guidance (DAG), inspired by Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG), to enhance semantic alignment while maintaining structural consistency. The paper demonstrates through quantitative and qualitative experiments that FlowDirector outperforms existing training-free baselines in instruction adherence, temporal coherence, and background preservation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of an inversion-free, flow-based editing paradigm to video. While flow-based techniques have been explored in image editing, adapting them to video presents unique challenges in maintaining temporal consistency. The SAFC mechanism for localized control is also a significant contribution, providing more fine-grained control than previous latent space modulation techniques. The DAG, an adaptation of CFG to the flow-based editing context, seems like a relatively minor adaptation of an existing idea, but its effectiveness and the problem it addresses enhance the novelty of the complete system.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to address the limitations of inversion-based video editing methods, particularly issues with temporal coherence and structural distortion. The method enables more precise and semantically faithful edits without requiring expensive training. This simplifies the process and makes it applicable to various video editing tasks, fostering improved content creation and manipulation. The performance gains reported in the quantitative and qualitative results showcase the efficacy of the proposed framework.

**Strengths:**

*   **Inversion-free approach:** Circumvents the inherent problems of inversion-based methods for video editing (temporal inconsistencies and structure degradation).
*   **Spatially Attentive Flow Correction (SAFC):** Provides explicit control over editable regions, ensuring better preservation of unedited areas and enhancing spatial consistency.
*   **Differential Averaging Guidance (DAG):** Increases semantic alignment while mitigating potential issues with structural preservation.
*   **Strong experimental results:** The paper demonstrates compelling quantitative and qualitative results, showcasing state-of-the-art performance compared to existing baselines across several evaluation metrics.
*   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is well-organized and provides a clear explanation of the proposed method and its components.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The integration of SAFC and DAG adds complexity to the framework. Although effective, the number of hyperparameters might make optimization and application to new scenarios more complex.
*   **Computational cost:** While it avoids inversion, flow-based methods can be computationally expensive, and iterative methods (such as the DAG strategy) compound this issue. Although the paper argues for DAG's efficiency compared to naive averaging, a thorough analysis of the trade-off between quality and computational cost would improve the robustness of their argument.
*   **Limited application domain?** The quantitative experiments focus on specific types of video editing scenarios. The paper could benefit from an analysis on which specific video structures are best suited to being edited with this algorithm compared to others.

**Potential Influence:**

FlowDirector has the potential to influence future research in text-driven video editing by establishing a new paradigm based on inversion-free, flow-based techniques. It could also encourage further exploration of attention-guided mechanisms and guidance-enhanced editing strategies for improving the quality and control of video editing processes. The approach is promising for its ability to avoid the limitations of existing latent-space-based techniques, and may inspire the development of methods that can be used in conjunction with diffusion, such as in the latent space, improving structural consistency in edits of diffusion videos.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

FlowDirector tackles a genuine problem within text-driven video editing by offering an inversion-free path. The performance gains are significant, but it's not completely revolutionary. The core strength comes from a clever combination of techniques to work around problems with the flow based approach. These include spatial attention to maintain parts of the image that should remain unaffected, and a differential averaging technique to improve convergence to the target space and semantic meaning. Overall the paper presents a significant, well tested and thoughtfully considered advancement.

### TALL -- A Trainable Architecture for Enhancing LLM Performance in Low-Resource Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05057v1)
- **Authors**: Moshe Ofer, Orel Zamler, Amos Azaria
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in high-resource languages but struggle with low-resource languages due to limited training data. This paper presents TALL (Trainable Architecture for Enhancing LLM Performance in Low-Resource Languages), which integrates an LLM with two bilingual translation models. TALL transforms low-resource inputs into high-resource representations, leveraging the LLM's capabilities while preserving linguistic features through dimension alignment layers and custom transformers. Our experiments on Hebrew demonstrate significant improvements over several baselines, including direct use, naive translation, and fine-tuning approaches. The architecture employs a parameter-efficient strategy, freezing pre-trained components while training only lightweight adapter modules, balancing computational efficiency with performance gains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TALL (Trainable Architecture for Enhancing LLM Performance in Low-Resource Languages), a novel architecture designed to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in low-resource languages. TALL integrates an LLM with two bilingual translation models, using trainable dimension alignment adapters and custom transformers to bridge the gap between low and high-resource language representations. The architecture transforms low-resource inputs into high-resource representations, leveraging the LLM's capabilities while preserving linguistic features. Experiments on Hebrew demonstrate significant improvements over baselines like direct use of LLMs, naive translation, and fine-tuning, with a parameter-efficient approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The architecture's core idea of integrating an LLM with translation models via alignment adapters demonstrates a moderate degree of novelty. The alignment adapters themselves build upon existing adapter techniques but are adapted specifically for the challenge of integrating information from translation models into the LLM space. The custom transformer component is also tailored to the specific needs of the TALL architecture and adds to the novelty. While not radically groundbreaking, the combination of these elements into a cohesive architecture is innovative.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a critical issue: the poor performance of LLMs in low-resource languages.  The experiments on Hebrew provide evidence that the TALL architecture can lead to substantial accuracy gains over existing methods, including fine-tuning and naive translation approaches. Demonstrating that linguistic knowledge embedded in high-resource LLMs can be effectively leveraged in low-resource settings is also significant. The parameter-efficient design is particularly valuable, as it makes the architecture computationally feasible for practical applications. However, the single token learning implementation limits the effectiveness to broad contextual dependencies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The core architecture is well-defined and clearly explained.
    *   The experiments provide solid empirical evidence of the architecture's effectiveness, with clear comparisons to relevant baselines.
    *   The parameter-efficient design is a significant advantage.
    *   The discussion of limitations is thorough, acknowledging potential weaknesses and outlining areas for future improvement.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on translation models could be a limitation in scenarios where high-quality translation models are unavailable.
    *   The single token learning signal implementation limits the effectiveness to broad contextual dependencies.
    *   The inference efficiency challenges due to the multiple translation and alignment modules must be taken into consideration.
    *   The error propagation could affect the final model prediction, as the architecture involves a multi-stage pipeline.

*   **Potential Impact:** If further developed and validated across more low-resource languages, TALL has the potential to become a valuable tool for improving NLP capabilities in resource-constrained settings. The parameter-efficient design makes it potentially deployable in scenarios where computational resources are limited.

*   **Justification:** The paper presents a well-engineered architecture and convincing experimental results that demonstrate the benefits of their approach. However, the single-token prediction limitation and dependence on existing translation models prevent a higher rating. The idea is solid and builds upon existing techniques but delivers notable improvements over existing baselines.

### A Survey on Vietnamese Document Analysis and Recognition: Challenges and Future Directions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05061v1)
- **Authors**: Anh Le, Thanh Lam, Dung Nguyen
- **Abstract**: Vietnamese document analysis and recognition (DAR) is a crucial field with applications in digitization, information retrieval, and automation. Despite advancements in OCR and NLP, Vietnamese text recognition faces unique challenges due to its complex diacritics, tonal variations, and lack of large-scale annotated datasets. Traditional OCR methods often struggle with real-world document variations, while deep learning approaches have shown promise but remain limited by data scarcity and generalization issues. Recently, large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models have demonstrated remarkable improvements in text recognition and document understanding, offering a new direction for Vietnamese DAR. However, challenges such as domain adaptation, multimodal learning, and computational efficiency persist. This survey provide a comprehensive review of existing techniques in Vietnamese document recognition, highlights key limitations, and explores how LLMs can revolutionize the field. We discuss future research directions, including dataset development, model optimization, and the integration of multimodal approaches for improved document intelligence. By addressing these gaps, we aim to foster advancements in Vietnamese DAR and encourage community-driven solutions.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper and provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance.

**Summary:**

The paper is a survey of Vietnamese Document Analysis and Recognition (DAR). It highlights the unique challenges posed by the Vietnamese language due to its complex diacritical system, tonal variations, and the relative scarcity of large, high-quality annotated datasets. The survey reviews traditional OCR methods and more recent deep learning-based approaches, including those used in competitions like VOHTR and the MC-OCR challenge. It also discusses post-processing techniques for improving OCR accuracy. A significant portion of the paper focuses on the potential role of Large Language Models (LLMs) in advancing Vietnamese DAR, while also acknowledging the current limitations in applying LLMs effectively, mainly due to the lack of pre-training data and high computational costs. Finally, the paper outlines future research directions, emphasizing dataset development, model robustness, and multimodal document understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive aggregation and synthesis of existing research specifically focusing on Vietnamese DAR. While individual techniques discussed are often adaptations of methods used for other languages, the paper provides a valuable overview of how these techniques have been applied and adapted for the unique characteristics of the Vietnamese language. The discussion on the current role and future potential of LLMs in this context is also relatively novel, although it leans heavily on the acknowledged limitations.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its role as a consolidating resource for researchers and practitioners in the field. It clearly articulates the key challenges, limitations, and potential research directions in Vietnamese DAR. It serves as a valuable starting point for those entering the field and provides a useful reference for those already working in it. The emphasis on the need for standardized datasets, benchmarks, and collaborative efforts is particularly important for fostering future progress.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey provides a broad overview of the Vietnamese DAR landscape, including traditional methods, deep learning approaches, and the emerging role of LLMs.
    *   **Clear Articulation of Challenges:** The paper effectively highlights the specific difficulties associated with Vietnamese OCR, such as diacritic handling, word segmentation, and dataset limitations.
    *   **Emphasis on Future Directions:** The discussion of future research directions provides valuable insights and guidance for researchers.
    *   **Well-Organized and Readable:** The paper is well-structured and written in a clear and accessible style, making it easy to understand even for readers not intimately familiar with the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth in Technical Analysis:** While the paper provides a good overview, it lacks in-depth technical analysis of specific algorithms and techniques. The descriptions tend to be high-level, without delving into the details of implementations and comparative performance analysis.
    *   **Over-Reliance on Acknowledged Limitations:** While acknowledging the limitations of LLMs is appropriate, the paper could have explored potential mitigation strategies or alternative approaches in more detail.
    *   **Limited Critical Comparative Analysis of Datasets:** While datasets are summarized, there is little in the way of analysis of the specific types of data present in different datasets, their strengths and weaknesses, and their fitness for specific tasks.
    *   **Future Directions are Broad rather than Specific:** The future directions are fairly broad and could have been significantly enhanced by suggestions on very specific implementations or types of future research.

**Justification for Score:**

Based on the above analysis, I assign a score of 6 to this paper.

While the paper is a valuable and comprehensive survey of Vietnamese DAR, its lack of in-depth technical analysis, over-reliance on acknowledged limitations, and generic recommendations for future research hold it back from being a truly exceptional contribution. It is, however, a useful resource for the community and serves as a good starting point for further research in the field. It is better than merely average as it highlights unique challenges and research direction for a language with specific characteristics, but lacks cutting-edge in terms of new methodological insights or a novel critical comparative perspective. The survey serves its purpose as a compilation of the field and introduction to new researchers.

**Score: 6**

- **Score**: 6/10

### Does It Make Sense to Speak of Introspection in Large Language Models?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05068v1)
- **Authors**: Iulia Comşa, Murray Shanahan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit compelling linguistic behaviour, and sometimes offer self-reports, that is to say statements about their own nature, inner workings, or behaviour. In humans, such reports are often attributed to a faculty of introspection and are typically linked to consciousness. This raises the question of how to interpret self-reports produced by LLMs, given their increasing linguistic fluency and cognitive capabilities. To what extent (if any) can the concept of introspection be meaningfully applied to LLMs? Here, we present and critique two examples of apparent introspective self-report from LLMs. In the first example, an LLM attempts to describe the process behind its own ``creative'' writing, and we argue this is not a valid example of introspection. In the second example, an LLM correctly infers the value of its own temperature parameter, and we argue that this can be legitimately considered a minimal example of introspection, albeit one that is (presumably) not accompanied by conscious experience.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Does It Make Sense to Speak of Introspection in Large Language Models?"

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether the concept of introspection, traditionally associated with conscious human minds, can be meaningfully applied to Large Language Models (LLMs). It proposes a lightweight definition of introspection as "accurately describing an internal state (or mechanism) of the LLM through a causal process that links the internal state (or mechanism) and the self-report in question." It then analyzes two case studies: an LLM describing its creative writing process and an LLM estimating its own sampling temperature. The authors argue that the first case is mere mimicry of human self-reports, while the second, where the LLM infers its temperature parameter, represents a minimal, but legitimate, example of introspection. They address critiques concerning the continuity of the "entity" performing introspection in LLMs and emphasize that focusing on the cognitive aspect of introspection can inform the design and transparency of future LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its attempt to bridge the gap between the philosophical concept of introspection and the functional capabilities of LLMs. While the idea of LLMs exhibiting "mind-like" properties isn't entirely new, the authors offer a relatively well-defined criterion for identifying a specific cognitive phenomenon. The emphasis on a causal connection between internal state and self-report is a good attempt to differentiate from mere mimicry.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is limited by its conceptual nature. It primarily provides a framework for thinking about LLM introspection rather than providing definitive empirical evidence. However, it is still significant in its attempt to provide some clarity to discussions regarding the complex capabilities of LLMs. It also offers a way to ground the discussion of consciousness by exploring specific functional aspects rather than the entire "package." The case study involving temperature estimation is quite compelling and provides a concrete example to work with.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear Definition: The paper provides a useful and clear definition of introspection tailored to LLMs.
    *   Case Studies: The case studies offer concrete examples, grounding the discussion in actual LLM behavior.
    *   Critical Analysis: The authors are careful to avoid over-interpretation and acknowledge the limitations of their analysis, particularly the issue of imitation.
    *   Addresses Key Critiques: The discussion of entity continuity and the role of the context window is an important contribution.
    * Links to Consciousness Research: Separating functional aspects of introspection from the more complex philosophical interpretations linked to consciousness can help make clearer assessments for future LLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Empirical Evidence: The paper is primarily conceptual, lacking robust empirical validation. The cases are illustrative but do not definitively prove introspection.
    *   Simplified Definition: The "lightweight" definition, while useful, might be seen as too simplistic by some. It might miss more nuanced aspects of human introspection.
    * Scope: While they attempt to address issues with continuity, a key weakness of the approach is the lack of continuous existence for LLMs.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a thought-provoking and timely conceptual analysis. While it lacks definitive empirical proof, it sets the stage for future investigations into the possibility of introspection within LLMs. The effort to clarify the concept and provide a framework for assessing it is valuable, particularly as LLMs become more sophisticated. However, the limitations regarding the lack of strong empirical support and the somewhat simplified definition prevent it from being considered a truly exceptional contribution.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reason-to-Recommend: Using Interaction-of-Thought Reasoning to Enhance LLM Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05069v1)
- **Authors**: Keyu Zhao, Fengli Xu, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Driven by advances in Large Language Models (LLMs), integrating them into recommendation tasks has gained interest due to their strong semantic understanding and prompt flexibility. Prior work encoded user-item interactions or metadata into prompts for recommendations. In parallel, LLM reasoning, boosted by test-time scaling and reinforcement learning, has excelled in fields like mathematics and code, where reasoning traces and correctness signals are clear, enabling high performance and interpretability. However, directly applying these reasoning methods to recommendation is ineffective because user feedback is implicit and lacks reasoning supervision. To address this, we propose $\textbf{R2Rec}$, a reasoning-enhanced recommendation framework that samples interaction chains from the user-item graph and converts them into structured interaction-of-thoughts via a progressive masked prompting strategy, with each thought representing stepwise reasoning grounded in interaction context. This allows LLMs to simulate step-by-step decision-making based on implicit patterns. We design a two-stage training pipeline: supervised fine-tuning teaches basic reasoning from high-quality traces, and reinforcement learning refines reasoning via reward signals, alleviating sparse explicit supervision. Experiments on three real-world datasets show R2Rec outperforms classical and LLM-based baselines with an average $\textbf{10.48%}$ improvement in HitRatio@1 and $\textbf{131.81%}$ gain over the original LLM. Furthermore, the explicit reasoning chains enhance interpretability by revealing the decision process. Our code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/R2Rec-7C5D.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Reason-to-Recommend: Using Interaction-of-Thought Reasoning to Enhance LLM Recommendation":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel framework, R2Rec, for enhancing Large Language Model (LLM)-based recommendation systems by incorporating interaction-of-thought reasoning. It addresses the challenge of transferring LLM reasoning capabilities (developed primarily for structured domains like mathematics and code) to the recommendation domain, where user feedback is implicit and lacks explicit reasoning supervision. R2Rec constructs interaction chains from user-item graphs and transforms them into structured reasoning chains using a progressive, masked prompting strategy. A two-stage training pipeline is employed: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to impart basic reasoning skills using annotated traces, and reinforcement learning (RL) to refine the reasoning process based on reward signals. Experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to both classical and LLM-based baselines. The paper also highlights the enhanced interpretability offered by the explicit reasoning chains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to incorporating structured reasoning into LLM-based recommendation systems. The key innovations are:

    *   **Interaction-of-Thought:** The concept of converting user-item interaction chains into interpretable reasoning traces via a carefully designed progressive prompting strategy is original. This addresses a core limitation of directly applying LLMs to recommendation tasks, where explicit reasoning data is scarce.
    *   **Reasoning-Augmented Training Pipeline:** The two-stage SFT and RL training procedure is effective. SFT provides a crucial warm start for the LLM to learn step-by-step inference, while RL enables self-improvement through reward-driven optimization.
    *   **Enhanced Interpretability:** By providing explicit reasoning chains behind recommendations, it offers insights into the decision-making process.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of LLM-based recommendation systems:

    *   **Performance Improvement:** The empirical results show a substantial improvement (10.48% on average in HitRatio@1) compared to strong baselines, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    *   **Addressing a Key Limitation:** It directly tackles the challenge of adapting LLM reasoning to the implicit nature of user feedback in recommendation.
    *   **Advancing Interpretability:** Providing explanations for recommendations is critical in building user trust and confidence. The explicit reasoning chains contribute to this goal.
    *   **Practical Applicability:** The method, although relying on LLMs, presents a pipeline that can be implemented and tested in real-world recommendation scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for a reasoning-enhanced framework.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The R2Rec framework is described in detail, with a clear explanation of each component (interaction chain construction, reasoning transformation, training pipeline).
    *   **Strong Empirical Evaluation:** The experiments are comprehensive, including comparisons to various baselines, ablation studies, transfer learning experiments, and case studies.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides insights into the contribution of each component through ablation and reward curve analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Context Length Limitation:** The paper acknowledges that the context length of LLMs restricts the number of interaction-of-thought sequences that can be processed simultaneously. This could limit the model's ability to capture long-range dependencies in user behavior. The authors mention retrieval-augmented prompting, suggesting that they're already aware of this constraint.
    *   **Computational cost:** Using an LLM for recommendation is computationally expensive in both the training and inference phases. This may limit its usability in some scenarios where resources are constrained.
    *   **Dataset dependency:** Despite the transfer learning experiments, it's still probable that the effectiveness is highly dependent on the characteristics of the dataset.
    *   **Dependence on Interaction Graph Quality:** The method relies on the quality and completeness of the user-item interaction graph. In scenarios with sparse or noisy data, the performance could be degraded.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper provides a promising direction for future research on LLM-based recommendation systems.
    *   It encourages the development of more interpretable and explainable recommendation models.
    *   The interaction-of-thought reasoning approach could be applied to other domains beyond recommendation.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates significant novelty by introducing a novel and effective framework for incorporating structured reasoning into LLM-based recommendation systems. The empirical results are compelling, and the analysis is thorough. However, the method faces challenges related to context length limitations, and dataset dependency, which could limit its scalability and generalization capabilities. Also, while the results are statistically significant, the gains in HitRatio@1, while noticeable, are still in a relatively small range. For these reasons, a perfect score is not justified, but the paper represents a significant advancement, paving the way for more powerful and interpretable LLM-based recommenders, and is deserving of a high rating.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### RIVAL: Reinforcement Learning with Iterative and Adversarial Optimization for Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05070v1)
- **Authors**: Tianjiao Li, Mengran Yu, Chenyu Shi, Yanjun Zhao, Xiaojing Liu, Qiang Zhang, Qi Zhang, Xuanjing Huang, Jiayin Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) possess strong multilingual capabilities, and combining Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) with translation tasks has shown great potential. However, we observe that this paradigm performs unexpectedly poorly when applied to colloquial subtitle translation tasks. In this work, we investigate this issue and find that the offline reward model (RM) gradually diverges from the online LLM due to distributional shift, ultimately leading to undesirable training outcomes. To address this, we propose RIVAL, an adversarial training framework that formulates the process as a min-max game between the RM and the LLM. RIVAL iteratively updates the both models, with the RM trained to distinguish strong from weak translations (qualitative preference reward), and the LLM trained to enhance its translation for closing this gap. To stabilize training and improve generalizability, we also incorporate quantitative preference reward (e.g., BLEU) into the RM, enabling reference-free quality modeling aligned with human evaluation. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that the proposed adversarial training framework significantly improves upon translation baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper addresses the problem of applying Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) to colloquial subtitle translation using large language models (LLMs).  The authors observe that directly applying RLHF in this context leads to poor performance due to distributional shift between the offline reward model (RM) and the online LLM.  The LLM tends to exploit weaknesses in the RM, generating text not present in the source to achieve high reward ("reward hacking"). To counter this, the paper proposes RIVAL (Reinforcement learning with Iterative and Adversarial Optimization), an adversarial training framework.  RIVAL frames the training process as a min-max game between the RM and the LLM. The RM is trained to distinguish between high-quality and low-quality translations (qualitative preference), while the LLM is trained to minimize the gap.  To stabilize training and improve generalizability, a quantitative preference reward (BLEU score approximation) is also incorporated into the RM. Experiments demonstrate that RIVAL significantly improves translation performance compared to baselines on a newly collected dataset of colloquial subtitles, without significantly degrading out-of-distribution performance.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper introduces a novel approach, RIVAL, that adapts adversarial training to the RLHF framework, specifically targeting the distributional shift issue observed in colloquial subtitle translation. While adversarial training is not new in general, its application to address this specific problem in RLHF for NMT seems relatively novel. The inclusion of a quantitative preference reward in addition to the qualitative aspect also adds to the novelty by addressing stability issues.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in the practical improvement of translation quality for a challenging and under-explored domain (colloquial subtitles). The identified problem of distributional shift in the standard RLHF setup for NMT is important and the proposed solution (RIVAL) demonstrates effective mitigation.  The new dataset contributes to the community, allowing for further research on this task. The findings regarding the instability introduced by solely qualitative preference rewards are also useful for the wider RLHF community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies and motivates the problem of distributional shift and reward hacking in the context of RLHF for colloquial subtitle translation.
    *   **Sound Methodology:** The adversarial training framework is well-reasoned and technically sound. The iterative updating of the RM and LLM is a logical way to address distributional shift. The additional of quantitative reward appears critical to improving stability. The implementation details seem thorough and enable reproducibility.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of RIVAL compared to strong baselines. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components of the framework (qualitative vs. quantitative rewards).
    *   **New Dataset:** Creating and releasing a high-quality dataset for colloquial subtitle translation is a valuable contribution in its own right.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation primarily focuses on Chinese-English translation. While WMT datasets are used for out-of-domain testing, more language pairs could strengthen the generalizability claims.
    *   **Lack of Direct Human Evaluation:**  Although the paper cites correlation between GPT4 and human evaluation, there is a lack of direct human evaluation in measuring translation quality. This could have provided more compelling evidence. The degree to which model's "hallucinations" (new text) align with what is acceptable for subtitling is also not deeply explored.
    *   **Complexity:**  The proposed adversarial framework does add complexity to the standard RLHF pipeline, which might limit its adoption in resource-constrained settings. The complexity justifies the value, but warrants a more simplified version if that is possible.
    *   **Clarity of Rationale:** While well-explained, certain design choices could use further clarification regarding the reasoning behind them.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in RLHF for NMT and potentially other NLG tasks. The adversarial training approach could be adopted and adapted to address similar distributional shift issues in other domains. The public dataset should also encourage further research on the topic of colloquial subtitle translation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field. The problem is important and well-motivated. The RIVAL framework is both novel and practically effective. There is room for improvement regarding human evaluation, scope of evaluation, and complexity, but overall, it makes a meaningful impact. The dataset and findings regarding stability can lead to other researchers building upon this paper.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Just a Scratch: Enhancing LLM Capabilities for Self-harm Detection through Intent Differentiation and Emoji Interpretation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05073v1)
- **Authors**: Soumitra Ghosh, Gopendra Vikram Singh, Shambhavi, Sabarna Choudhury, Asif Ekbal
- **Abstract**: Self-harm detection on social media is critical for early intervention and mental health support, yet remains challenging due to the subtle, context-dependent nature of such expressions. Identifying self-harm intent aids suicide prevention by enabling timely responses, but current large language models (LLMs) struggle to interpret implicit cues in casual language and emojis. This work enhances LLMs' comprehension of self-harm by distinguishing intent through nuanced language-emoji interplay. We present the Centennial Emoji Sensitivity Matrix (CESM-100), a curated set of 100 emojis with contextual self-harm interpretations and the Self-Harm Identification aNd intent Extraction with Supportive emoji sensitivity (SHINES) dataset, offering detailed annotations for self-harm labels, casual mentions (CMs), and serious intents (SIs). Our unified framework: a) enriches inputs using CESM-100; b) fine-tunes LLMs for multi-task learning: self-harm detection (primary) and CM/SI span detection (auxiliary); c) generates explainable rationales for self-harm predictions. We evaluate the framework on three state-of-the-art LLMs-Llama 3, Mental-Alpaca, and MentalLlama, across zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuned scenarios. By coupling intent differentiation with contextual cues, our approach commendably enhances LLM performance in both detection and explanation tasks, effectively addressing the inherent ambiguity in self-harm signals. The SHINES dataset, CESM-100 and codebase are publicly available at: https://www.iitp.ac.in/~ai-nlp-ml/resources.html#SHINES .
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel framework for enhancing the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to detect self-harm on social media by focusing on intent differentiation and emoji interpretation. The authors present two key contributions: the Centennial Emoji Sensitivity Matrix (CESM-100), a curated set of emojis with contextual self-harm interpretations, and the Self-Harm Identification aNd intent Extraction with Supportive emoji sensitivity (SHINES) dataset, offering annotations for self-harm labels, casual mentions (CMs), and serious intents (SIs). The framework enriches inputs using CESM-100, fine-tunes LLMs for multi-task learning (self-harm detection and CM/SI span detection), and generates explainable rationales for self-harm predictions. The framework is evaluated on Llama 3, Mental-Alpaca, and MentalLlama across various scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents incremental novelty. The idea of using LLMs for self-harm detection is not entirely new, but the specific focus on differentiating casual mentions from serious intent using emoji context is a worthwhile contribution.  The CESM-100 is a useful resource, but its impact depends on its comprehensive nature and the extent to which it truly captures the nuances of emoji usage in self-harm contexts. The SHINES dataset is also valuable but the size may be a limitation for extensive training of large language models, although augmentation addresses the limitations.
* **Significance:**  Self-harm detection is a crucial task for mental health support, making this work relevant. The findings demonstrate that incorporating intent differentiation and emoji context can improve LLM performance in this domain, which could lead to more effective early intervention strategies. The explainable rationales are also a positive addition, promoting trust and understanding of the model's predictions. The practical impact hinges on the scalability and generalizability of the approach across diverse social media platforms and user demographics.
* **Strengths:**
    * The paper addresses a challenging and important problem.
    * The proposed framework is well-structured and clearly explained.
    * The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.
    * The introduction of the CESM-100 and SHINES dataset are valuable contributions.
    * The explainable rationales enhance transparency and trust.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The size of the SHINES dataset may limit the generalizability of the results.
    * The reliance on Reddit data introduces potential platform bias.
    * The performance improvements, while positive, might not be substantial enough to warrant widespread adoption, particularly considering the computational cost of fine-tuning large LLMs.
    * The dataset creation process with synthetic posts could lead to bias or overfitting.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of mental health informatics and contribute to the development of more accurate and reliable self-harm detection systems. The CESM-100 and SHINES dataset could serve as valuable resources for future research in this area. However, more extensive validation and real-world deployment studies are needed to fully assess the impact of this work.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

While the paper demonstrates clear improvements in self-harm detection by incorporating emoji context and intent differentiation, it doesn't represent a breakthrough that fundamentally changes the landscape. The novelty is primarily in the specific combination of techniques and the creation of new resources (CESM-100 and SHINES). The reliance on a single platform (Reddit) and the limited size of the SHINES dataset are concerns. The practical significance is also somewhat limited by the relatively modest performance gains and the need for further validation in real-world scenarios.

- **Score**: 6/10

### SeedEdit 3.0: Fast and High-Quality Generative Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05083v1)
- **Authors**: Peng Wang, Yichun Shi, Xiaochen Lian, Zhonghua Zhai, Xin Xia, Xuefeng Xiao, Weilin Huang, Jianchao Yang
- **Abstract**: We introduce SeedEdit 3.0, in companion with our T2I model Seedream 3.0 [22], which significantly improves over our previous version [27] in both aspects of edit instruction following and image content (e.g., ID/IP) preservation on real image inputs. Additional to model upgrading with T2I, in this report, we present several key improvements. First, we develop an enhanced data curation pipeline with a meta-info paradigm and meta-info embedding strategy that help mix images from multiple data sources. This allows us to scale editing data effectively, and meta information is helpfult to connect VLM with diffusion model more closely. Second, we introduce a joint learning pipeline for computing a diffusion loss and a reward loss. Finally, we evaluate SeedEdit 3.0 on our testing benchmarks, for real image editing, where it achieves a best trade-off between multiple aspects, yielding a high usability rate of 56.1%, compared to SeedEdit 1.6 (38.4%), GPT4o (37.1%) and Gemini 2.0 (30.3%).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SeedEdit 3.0, an improved generative image editing system built upon the Seedream 3.0 text-to-image model. It claims significant advancements in edit instruction following and image content preservation compared to its previous version. The key improvements highlighted are: a refined data curation pipeline leveraging meta-information and a joint learning pipeline incorporating both diffusion and reward losses.  The paper presents quantitative evaluations on internal benchmarks, showing a better trade-off between usability, instruction following, and image quality compared to competing models like SeedEdit 1.6, GPT-40, and Gemini 2.0, along with being significantly faster than GPT-40. The paper details its data curation, model architecture, training strategies (including diffusion with reward models and joint training with T2I), and inference efficiency techniques (distillation, CFG distillation, and adaptive timestep sampling).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the paper claims significant improvements, the described components build largely upon existing techniques. The meta-information data curation pipeline and the joint learning pipeline are presented as key innovations, but they appear to be more of an engineering effort of combining well-known techniques (VLM, reward models, data augmentation) rather than groundbreaking algorithmic advancements. Using meta-info for data is not novel, and joint training with reward models is also established practice. The improvements in inference efficiency using distillation and adaptive sampling, while important, are also incremental enhancements to existing distillation methods. The main "novelty" lies in the specific combination and application of these methods to the Seedream architecture.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is moderate. The reported improvements in usability, instruction following, and image quality over existing methods (including commercial models like GPT-40 and Gemini 2.0) are practically relevant. A fast and high-quality image editing system has clear applications. However, because the components described are built upon established techniques, the potential for a transformative impact on the field is limited. The paper presents evaluations using internal benchmarks. It is difficult to evaluate the generalisability of the model.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.
    *   Detailed description of the data curation pipeline and training strategies.
    *   Quantitative evaluations showing improvements over baseline methods.
    *   Practical focus on inference efficiency.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited algorithmic novelty. The methods are primarily combinations of existing techniques.
    *   Evaluations are primarily on internal benchmarks, which limits the generality of the results, especially with the internal label mentioned.
    *   The paper doesn't fully delve into the limitations of each module.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's impact will likely be within the realm of practical applications. It could be valuable for practitioners and engineers interested in building similar image editing systems, showcasing a viable approach for combining various techniques. But it's unlikely to dramatically shift the research direction of the field due to its limited algorithmic novelty.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the moderate novelty (mostly incremental improvements) and reasonable significance (practical application), a score in the middle range seems appropriate.  The improvements are valuable but not disruptive. A lot depends on the user cases. Also the internal benchmark also hinders the score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Astraea: A GPU-Oriented Token-wise Acceleration Framework for Video Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05096v1)
- **Authors**: Haosong Liu, Yuge Cheng, Zihan Liu, Aiyue Chen, Yiwu Yao, Chen Chen, Jingwen Leng, Yu Feng, Minyi Guo
- **Abstract**: Video diffusion transformers (vDiTs) have made impressive progress in text-to-video generation, but their high computational demands present major challenges for practical deployment. While existing acceleration methods reduce workload at various granularities, they often rely on heuristics, limiting their applicability. We introduce ASTRAEA, an automatic framework that searches for near-optimal configurations for vDiT-based video generation. At its core, ASTRAEA proposes a lightweight token selection mechanism and a memory-efficient, GPU-parallel sparse attention strategy, enabling linear reductions in execution time with minimal impact on generation quality. To determine optimal token reduction for different timesteps, we further design a search framework that leverages a classic evolutionary algorithm to automatically determine the distribution of the token budget effectively. Together, ASTRAEA achieves up to 2.4x inference speedup on a single GPU with great scalability (up to 13.2x speedup on 8 GPUs) while retaining better video quality compared to the state-of-the-art methods (<0.5% loss on the VBench score compared to the baseline vDiT models).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "ASTRAEA: A GPU-Oriented Token-wise Acceleration Framework for Video Diffusion Transformers" introduces a novel framework to speed up video generation using video diffusion transformers (vDiTs).  The core idea is to selectively compute only the "important" tokens at each denoising step. ASTRAEA employs a lightweight token selection mechanism based on a significance metric and a penalty term to discourage repeatedly selecting the same tokens. It also introduces a GPU-parallel sparse attention strategy.  To determine the optimal token budget allocation across timesteps, the framework employs an evolutionary algorithm to search for near-optimal configurations. The authors claim significant inference speedups on single and multi-GPU setups while preserving video quality, outperforming state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits a moderate degree of novelty. Token selection itself isn't entirely new, with prior work like ToCa exploring this area. However, the authors present a *specific combination* of techniques (lightweight selection metric, GPU-parallel sparse attention, and EA-based budget allocation) designed explicitly to address limitations of previous token-based acceleration methods, particularly concerning GPU overhead. A key contribution is the awareness of GPU memory and compute constraints. The "counterintuitive" sparse attention computation, focusing on selective query computation, is a novel aspect, as is the carefully crafted selection metric combining significance and penalty terms. The idea of using Evolutionary Algorithm (EA) is not new but applying it on token scheduling for vDiTs is a valid exploration.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of text-to-video generation: the high computational cost of vDiTs.  Speeding up inference is essential for practical deployment. The results demonstrate significant speedups with minimal quality degradation, making ASTRAEA a potentially valuable tool for practitioners. The design choices focusing on GPU-friendliness and parallelization are significant because they are practically relevant for real-world deployments. Demonstrating good scalability is also valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Evaluation:**  The paper provides thorough experimental results on two popular vDiT models (Wan and OpenSora) with comprehensive benchmarks, including VBench scores, PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS, latency, memory consumption, and FLOPs. The ablations and sensitivity studies are helpful in understanding the impact of different components.
    *   **GPU-Aware Design:** The ASTRAEA framework is explicitly designed to be GPU-friendly, addressing the memory and compute bottlenecks often encountered in previous token-wise methods.
    *   **Scalability:**  The results demonstrate good scalability across multiple GPUs, further increasing its practical value.
    *   **Competitive Results:**  ASTRAEA achieves better or comparable video quality compared to state-of-the-art methods while delivering substantial speedups.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the experimental results are convincing, the paper lacks deep theoretical justification for the design choices of the selection metric and the EA configuration.
    *   **Search Time:** The EA requires a significant search time (82 GPU hours on average), which could be a barrier to use for new models or datasets. The authors acknowledge this limitation. A non-stochastic solution to replace EA would be valuable.
    *   **Generality:**  While the authors claim generality, the experiments are conducted on a specific set of vDiT models and datasets. The effectiveness of ASTRAEA on other architectures or data distributions needs further validation. The reliance on the EA to adapt raises concerns about how well it will transfer to new scenarios.
    *   **Comparison against other state-of-the-art methods:** The paper compares against several previous methods that accelerate video generation. It should have explored some latest method such as [20] Weijie Kong, Qi Tian, Zijian Zhang, Rox Min, Zuozhuo Dai, Jin Zhou, Jiangfeng Xiong, Xin Li, Bo Wu, Jianwei Zhang, et al. Hunyuanvideo: A systematic framework for large video generative models. arXiv preprint arXiv:2412.03603, 2024.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7/10**.

*   The paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing a crucial problem (inference speed) in a practically relevant way (GPU-friendly design). The empirical results are compelling and demonstrate significant speedups with minimal quality loss. The specific combination of techniques provides a unique angle compared to previous token-based acceleration methods.
*   However, the long search time of the EA, the limited theoretical analysis, and the need for more extensive validation on different models and datasets limit its overall impact. Furthermore, ASTRAEA is not the first to explore the overall theme of token pruning, which reduces the novelty.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Membership Inference Attacks on Sequence Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05126v1)
- **Authors**: Lorenzo Rossi, Michael Aerni, Jie Zhang, Florian Tramèr
- **Abstract**: Sequence models, such as Large Language Models (LLMs) and autoregressive image generators, have a tendency to memorize and inadvertently leak sensitive information. While this tendency has critical legal implications, existing tools are insufficient to audit the resulting risks. We hypothesize that those tools' shortcomings are due to mismatched assumptions. Thus, we argue that effectively measuring privacy leakage in sequence models requires leveraging the correlations inherent in sequential generation. To illustrate this, we adapt a state-of-the-art membership inference attack to explicitly model within-sequence correlations, thereby demonstrating how a strong existing attack can be naturally extended to suit the structure of sequence models. Through a case study, we show that our adaptations consistently improve the effectiveness of memorization audits without introducing additional computational costs. Our work hence serves as an important stepping stone toward reliable memorization audits for large sequence models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of membership inference attacks (MIAs) against sequence models, specifically Large Language Models (LLMs) and autoregressive image generators. It argues that existing MIA methods, particularly the Likelihood Ratio Attack (LiRA), underperform in this context because they fail to account for the inherent correlations between sequential elements.
